#2922. Fantasy Football Rankings: Hayden Winks' QB/TE/DEF/K Blueprint for Week 9

The Blueprint is my weekly column and basically everything that I use to build out the most accurate rankings I can. There will be an RB column, a WR column and this combination column on the onesie positions. You will find film notes and endless charts. Maybe enough charts to stress test the Yahoo website. I kid.

It's impossible to get every ranking thoroughly early in the week, so be sure to keep checking in on these columns through Sunday morning. As always, my full rankings at every position are available.

Burrow has been dealing as the season-long QB2 (26.2 PPG), generating +0.23 EPA per dropback (QB5). He is lighting up box scores through the air, leading the league in passing yards per game (302) and passing touchdowns per game (2.9). Cincinnati is leaning all the way into his arm, operating with a league-high 66% neutral pass rate (first).

That aggressive approach meets an Atlanta defense allowing 16.3 half PPR points per game to quarterbacks (23rd), where recent signal-callers like Tyler Huntley (24.0 points) and Caleb Williams (16.5 points) have put up functional days. The Bengals head in as 3-point road favorites with an implied team total of 27.25 points (sixth).

We'll see if Mike Evans (Achilles) can return, but it doesn't sound like Deebo Samuel Sr. will be back.

Purdy has been surgical as fantasy's QB4 (21.7 PPG), leading all qualified quarterbacks with +0.31 EPA per dropback (QB1). Over his last four starts against quality opponents, his efficiency has cooled to +0.03 EPA per dropback (16th) while averaging 17.3 fantasy points per game. His Week 7 outing without his starting receivers was his worst game of the year (16.0).

He draws a dream matchup against a Raiders defense that's allowing the fourth-most half PPR points per game to QBs (21.8). Las Vegas sits at the top right of the intersection between success rate and explosive rate allowed on dropbacks alongside the worst defenses in the NFL. It's allowed four straight QBs to finish as top-12 QBs. The 49ers enter as 9.5-point home favorites with a slate-leading implied team total of 30.5 points (first).

Allen's 28.2 fantasy points per game is currently ahead of the all-time fantasy record at QB (Lamar Jackson, 2024). He's done so without DJ Moore for half of the season. Moore was used properly last week with unlimited run-away routes, and then Dalton Kincaid found space downfield in multi-TE sets. It's a great formula.

The Vikings have been by far the best fantasy QB defense, however, allowing just 10.4 fantasy points per game. That's insane. Truly. They haven't faced an elite dual-threat QB this season, though.

Allen will be blitzed at a career-high rate, most likely. He's the QB5 in EPA per dropback against the blitz (+0.07), but that is far lower than on non-blitzes (+0.21, QB1). When blitzed, Allen has a 3.4% scramble rate compared to a 9% scramble rate when not blitzed. It's possible the Bills run a bit more often because of the pressure, as opposing offenses to Minnesota only pass 45% of the time in neutral situations (31st).

Buffalo enters as 3.5-point road favorites carrying an implied team total of 25.0 points (11th). It would make sense for Allen's fantasy output to take a hit here.

Mahomes was quite good last week considering the circumstances, finishing as the QB5 against a healthy Broncos defense. Across the year, Mahomes is the QB14 on QB12 EPA per dropback. He was slowed down by the Seahawks without Rashee Rice in Week 7, but the Chiefs have been one of the best offenses in the NFL when not facing a top-three defense this year. Their weaknesses at LT and WR are hidden best against mediocre opponents.

Insert the Jets, who may not have their top-two defensive linemen here. The Jets just allowed top-five fantasy outings from Kirk Cousins, Malik Willis and Drake Maye in their last three outings. It's obviously a great spot here. Kansas City enters as 9.5-point home favorites with a 28.75-point implied team total (third).

Williams is simply way more accurate this year. He's the QB2 in completion percentage over expected when adjusting for his throw depth. He's middle of the pack in sack rate (7.6%). And he's the QB4 in interception rate. Williams is seeing it extremely well, despite already facing the Vikings, Seahawks, Patriots and the feisty Falcons defenses. He's the QB3 in EPA per dropback, and he'll start spiking fantasy outings once he's pushed by the opponent.

The Bucs defense will be another tough defense to beat. They're fifth-best against fantasy QBs, and from Week 3 on, they've been the best defense in EPA per play, allowing just 15.8 points per game. They slowed down Dak Prescott in Week 5, then completely shut down Bryce Young and Michael Penix Jr. in the past two weeks. They have better pass-rushing depth and have enjoyed full breakouts from CBs Zyon McCollum and especially Jacob Parrish. Tampa Bay also wants to grind out the clock with a run game that is breaking my brain. Chicago returns home as 5.5-point home favorites sporting a 26.5-point implied team total (seventh).

The Goff outlier dud happened last week, finishing as the QB21 against the Vikings' chaotic defense. Despite that, Goff is the QB4 in EPA per dropback and pacing for 4,770 yards with 36 passing touchdowns. He's the QB6 in fantasy despite not having anything to show with his legs.

The Week 9 environment is pristine against a Miami defense allowing 20.8 half PPR points per game to QBs (sixth). Opposing quarterbacks have moved the ball against the Dolphins regularly, including recent outings from Joe Burrow (24.6 points), Geno Smith (23.5 points) and Drake Maye (22.4 points). Miami is struggling on play action and throws over the middle. That's the entire Lions offense. Detroit is listed as 7-point road favorites with a 28.75-point implied team total (third).

He's been the QB14 in EPA per dropback throughout the season, but timely scrambles and plenty of volume have made up for the efficiency decline. We'll see if A.J. Brown and Hunter Henry can suit up later, but Maye made it work with backups against the Dolphins last week (QB7).

He now faces a Packers defense that has kept QBs in check with a chaotic defensive style, surrendering just 14.9 half PPR points per game to the position (27th). Green Bay recently held Bryce Young to 12.4 points, Jared Goff to 18.0 and Dak Prescott to 15.0. The Patriots head into the matchup as 3-point home favorites with an implied team total of 25.25 points (10th).

It hasn't been the best Prescott season, and the Cowboys' run game has been so bad that they've lost some inexcusable games along the way. The good news is Prescott has healthy WR and OL units right now, and his defense looks as pathetic as usual. He's been the QB13 in fantasy points per game this year on just QB8 EPA per dropback. It's been much higher than that for long stretches, and I can see that coming back after a recent stretch of games against BAL, HOU, TB and GB. Prescott was the QB6 and QB11 in the last two games against softer opponents.

The Colts defense is one of the worst in the NFL, possibly the worst without DT DeForest Buckner. They were smoked by the Jaguars last week after firing their GM in late October. Dallas heads on the road as 6-point road favorites with a healthy implied team total of 28.5 points (fourth). There are truly no excuses here.

Jayden Daniels has been the QB15, QB3, QB7 and QB16 in his healthiest games. Since returning from an elbow dislocation, the Commanders have yet to call a designed run for him. He'll still scramble like a madman, but more of those are resulting in slides and running out of bounds early, too. This will hurt him on the margins, though he's still the QB1 in rushing yards per game (47.2).

The Rams present a fun matchup dynamic, ranking 21st in half PPR points allowed to opposing QBs (17.0 per game) but fourth in points allowed specifically to QB rushing. Opposing offenses facing Los Angeles also feature a 47% neutral pass rate, the third-lowest rate allowed in the NFL. Washington hosts this contest as 5.5-point home underdogs carrying a 21.0-point implied team total (23rd).

10. Trevor Lawrence (at BAL) - 18.8 proj. points

Lawrence is up to QB2 overall in EPA per dropback. He hasn't been pushed many times this year, so he's left fourth quarters without adding to his totals. He's only the QB16 in yards per game, leaving him with QB8 fantasy points per game. That's closer to his floor while everyone is healthy on offense. His ceiling is far higher when games are tighter.

That's the issue with the Thursday Night Game against third-string QB Joe Fagnano. The Ravens are 10-point dogs with a team total below 15 points. The Ravens defense is far more talented and far better coached than their current No. 15 ranking in fantasy points allowed to QBs, too. Nice field position and the occasional 1-on-1 loss downfield with Marlon Humphrey in coverage are enough to combat these minor risks for T-Law as a low-end QB1. The Jaguars are ninth in team total (24.75).

Shough is off the bye and it's possible WR Jordyn Tyson makes his debut and LT Kelvin Banks (ankle) returns after a prolonged absence. Those would be major upgrades. Shough's been the QB7 in fantasy points per game but was exposed versus the Vikings and Steelers recently while dealing with a broken non-throwing hand. He should be largely over that now, and the added rushing element has made him a low-end QB1 at times in the fastest-paced offense:

Week 9 presents a much tighter test against Cleveland, as the Browns rank 26th in half PPR points allowed to opposing QBs (15.2 per game). They haven't been tested by above-average QBs this entire month, however, with Aaron Rodgers, Cam Ward, Tyler Huntley and Geno Smith in their recent history. The Browns play man coverage more than anyone else, but the Saints have the WRs to hang in there. New Orleans is listed as a 2.5-point home favorite with a 23-point team total (14th).

Love is playing the sport like nobody else. His absurd aggressiveness and arm talent are amazing to watch every week.

Love has turned into the QB9 on a per-game basis, with the second-most yards per game (281). His top three pass catchers are healthy, and they're starting to figure out the best starting OL and ways to hide them on the margins. He's scored multiple touchdowns in 7 of 8 starts. Even with Josh Jacobs in the fold, the Packers have maintained their pass-first identity. That's how it should be behind this OL.

His Week 9 draw is tougher on paper against a New England defense that ranks 22nd in half PPR points allowed to quarterbacks (16.6). The Packers head on the road as 3-point road underdogs with an implied team total of 22.25 points (17th). That's lower than normal.

The Dolphins are definitely better in November than in September. This is the youngest team in the NFL, and it's a new scheme with a new QB. The team is gelling, while also getting healthier at OL and WR. Willis has been fantasy's QB7 since Week 5 on QB14 EPA per dropback. Some of that is because of a Caleb Douglas sideline YAC TD, but him being in the lineup is helpful! Miami is still 30th in neutral pass rate (44%), but they'll be forced to pass here.

The Lions have allowed the most fantasy points to QBs, and opposing offenses have the third-highest neutral pass rate allowed. Their run defense is simply better than their secondary. Detroit will miss star LB Jack Campbell over the middle (and I really hope things progress for him, beyond football), but they did get star S Brian Branch back recently. The Lions have given up the seventh-most rushing QB points this year, partially because of garbage time scrambles. Those count for us! Willis will try to continue this top-12 QB streak, after Kyler Murray (QB10), Jordan Love (QB12), Jacoby Brissett (QB7) and Bryce Young (QB7) have done just that this month. Miami carries a 21.75-point implied team total (18th) as 7-point home underdogs.

Stafford doesn't look fully healthy right now. Some of his sideline and downfield attempts lack his usual zip, and his throwing motion is feeling a little more Philip Rivers-like. I wasn't surprised to see Monday's report that his elbow is sore. That's what his throwing motion looks like right now, and the offense being extremely quick-hitting is further evidence of just that. Over the past month, Stafford's average depth of target is at just 5.4 yards downfield. The good news is the Rams have remained top-five in neutral pass rate in the two games without Davante Adams. Stafford is a lower-ceiling QB2 right now. He is fantasy's QB16 this year on QB16 EPA per dropback.

He now faces a Washington defense allowing the third-most half PPR points per game to quarterbacks (22.0). Opposing passers have found plenty of success against the Commanders, who rank sixth in QB passing points allowed per game. Jameis Winston tagged them for 326 yards and three passing scores in Week 5, while Brock Purdy posted 17.4 fantasy points the following week with backups at WR. The Rams enter as 5.5-point road favorites with a 26.5-point implied team total (eighth).

#2920. Santoli: AI bull market almost surely needs tech stocks to recover to keep advancing

The road to bull-market glory does not run through Walmart and Berkshire Hathaway and Eli Lilly.

There's been a virtual buying panic in traditionally defensive, high-quality stocks over the past few weeks, a near-equal-and-opposite offset to the selling pressure in semiconductors and AI-build-out spenders. The three stocks above — the only members of the $1 trillion market-cap club not in Big Tech – have collectively added $550 billion in market value in the past month, almost making up for what Alphabet has lost.

The Nasdaq-100 and the S&P 500 Low Volatility ETF (SPLV) are rushing toward one another in a satisfying picture of symmetry that nonetheless reflects an almost comical level of tactical rotation executed by an investment community accustomed to playing one factor against another.

While this to-and-fro has so far protected the S&P 500 as a whole from an appreciable setback for months, it has left it stymied around levels that date back to May — before we got two earnings-reporting seasons showing 30% annual profit growth.

A week ago here, I suggested the AI theme was nearing a make-or-break moment to stage a recovery or risk surrendering its leadership status and destabilizing the broader market. Thus far, this trade has failed to respond to moderate oversold conditions or a run of cosmetically strong hyperscaler quarterly results, further loosening its grip on the benefit of the doubt.

In stressed markets undergoing soul-searching about the long-term bullish thesis, prices often need to travel further to find buyers with conviction while the market action must disappoint dip-buyers to extinguish complacency. Hard to know if we're there, but presumably we're now closer.

The Nasdaq-100 closed Monday right at its 200-day moving average, off almost 11% from its record high. After an enervating failed Monday-morning bounce attempt, the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index finished down another 3.8% and settled under the late-July low that was set as the Situational Awareness hedge fund was finished liquidating.

"Markets did not get the memo that bearish seasonal pressure typically subsides in mid-October and instead ended the month with some of the most acute selling pressure that has been realized during the late-summer/early-fall period," notes JPMorgan technical strategist Jason Hunter. He's advising clients to remain on the defensive "until we see clear evidence of a bottoming process on the charts or until there are clear signs of capitulation."

This is a time-tested playbook, though many a sharp market pullback has ended without what would qualify as unqualified capitulation.

The market action is winning some plaudits for the way non-tech stocks have held up better, many groups benefiting from the "quality" rotation while abandoned consumer cyclicals get a reprieve from easing oil prices and the slide in the 10-year Treasury yield back below 5%.

This gives active managers plenty to do in playing against a highly concentrated and tech-dependent S&P 500. But I'll continue to argue that the bull market built on the AI story will almost surely need the AI story to get back on track to keep advancing. A prolonged period when the equal-weight S&P 500 outperforms the market-cap-weighted version is otherwise known – usually – as a bear market.

The AI-related stocks' weighting in the S&P 500 is off the highs but remains near 45%, according to Morgan Stanley.

As the chart shows, AI has wobbled relative to the rest of the market during this uptrend before reasserting itself.  But there's a trickier element to the current gut check: In prior phases of the buildout, the key limiting bottleneck gave investors something to buy in order to exploit it.

The initial scarcity of GPUs meant to own Nvidia above all else from 2023 through 2024. When cloud capacity, power and cooling were found to be the crucial scarce resources, hyperscaler stocks worked and the AI-adjacent industrials were drastically revalued. Then memory emerged as the chokepoint and Micron and Sandisk went stratospheric.

Now, as Nvidia's Jensen Huang has said, capital is the constraint and that not only leaves no asset to buy, but it raises the cost of capital broadly as the mega-caps go free-cash-flow-negative.

Overlapping feedback loops and scrambled correlations across the markets and the real economy have further sapped investor conviction. Treasury yields no longer move with oil but largely with AI-capex sentiment, with both in retreat lately. AI-stock setbacks also mute inflation momentum and the equity wealth effect, meaning fewer Federal Reserve rate hikes are anticipated now versus a month ago.

This all probably sounds like an elaborate trap, and it has become one for the previously crowded trades. Could this reflect the end game for the AI-exuberance market chapter, which as I keep pointing out peaked around the SpaceX IPO in June? Impossible to say for sure, but if it were, I'm not sure things would look much different to this point.

But I don't want to dismiss the possibility that a lot of the known challenges are largely reflected in prices.

I argued in August that we should assume the valuation peak for this cycle was in. We've now retrenched to a forward P/E of 18.5 on the S&P 500 from 23 a year ago and the Nasdaq-100 at 20x is at the slimmest premium to the S&P 500 valuation in nine years. I'm on board with the idea that the market is telling us companies are now "over-earning," but if nothing else there is less valuation risk now than in recent months.

Sentiment toward AI stocks is in the dumps. Funds' positioning in tech has retreated below neutral. Important trading lows this year have happened near the turn of the month on March 30 and July 29, and the Nasdaq-100's relative-strength reading is exactly as oversold as it was at those turns.

So, stay alert for possible relief bounces that could evolve into something more consequential if indeed the midterm elections pass in a way that allows traders to unclench a bit into the year's home stretch.

John Kolovos of Macro Risk Advisors on the latest reading: "Similar to valuation, sentiment is not a timing tool, but it is getting harder and harder to find any bulls, as a near-perfect storm of tight financial conditions is being met with tech earnings that have gone from bad to worse in the blink of an eye."

-Another good big-picture conversation on the practical business and investment implications of the next phase of AI proliferation on Patrick O'Shaughnessy's Invest Like the Best podcast, this one with investor Cliff Sosin. His comparison of AI infrastructure economics to internet connectivity providers and his argument for how most of the productivity boost will go toward "consumer surplus" rather than corporate profits are particularly interesting.

-For my birthday Monday, my colleague and friend Sara Eisen wondered how I would write a birthday message to myself. I immediately regretted suggesting she ask ChatGPT, which generated this credible and somewhat embarrassing result:

"Another year older, though the longer-term trend remains constructive. Some modest deterioration in the underlying internals, perhaps, but nothing that suggests a decisive breakdown.

"The candles on the cake are approaching historically elevated levels, though valuation concerns have persisted for years without derailing the advance. At this stage, the key question isn't whether I've gotten older — that's largely priced in — but whether the wisdom gains can continue to offset the inevitable compression in energy multiples.

"For now, I'm maintaining a cautiously optimistic outlook, with upside optionality, downside support from family and friends, and no immediate need for Fed intervention. Happy birthday to me. The secular bull market remains intact."

The stampede into perceived defensive stocks has been a crucial driver of Apple shares' massive outperformance over the Nasdaq-100 since the AI-capex agita flared up months ago. Apple heading into Monday's earnings report had outperformed the Nasdaq-100 by nearly 20 percentage points over the prior six months.

Its singular refusal to join the aggressive spending binge to build AI capacity preserved its free cash flow and made it the perfect counterweight to the capex trade, while inflating Apple's valuation to a 15-year high.

But its advantage in free-cash-flow yield has now narrowed, thanks to its stock-price gains and the collapse of peer shares.

#2919. Oil prices rise as investors weigh renewed U.S.-Iran conflict risks after midterm elections

Oil prices rose Friday as investors weighed the risk of renewed military escalation between the U.S. and Iran following the midterm elections, alongside prospects for a diplomatic breakthrough.

Brent crude futures climbed 1.67% to $99.90 per barrel, while U.S. West Texas Intermediate crude futures gained 1.43% to $91.69 per barrel.

The gains came as investors assessed whether the conclusion of the U.S. midterm elections could pave the way for renewed actions against Iran, potentially threatening oil supplies from the Middle East.

Bob McNally, Rapidan Energy Group founder and president, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" that he sees an 80% probability of military fighting resuming, forecasting that both oil benchmarks could climb above $100 a barrel.

However, hopes for a diplomatic resolution have also emerged after Iran reportedly sent a nuclear proposal to Washington through Qatar, Reuters reported, citing a senior Iranian official.

Iran reported three additional attacks on oil tankers in the Strait of Hormuz on Thursday, while data from the UK Maritime Trade Operations (UKMTO) indicated that attacks on vessels were continuing at a near-record rate.

"President Donald Trump is determined, as a legacy item, almost at all costs, to prevent Iran from getting a nuclear weapon, having them give up their nuclear capabilities," McNally said.

"The Iranian regime, on the other hand, is not willing to do it. And as long as that core difference in that deal space is absent, the escalation dynamic remains in place," he added.

McNally's view is shared by other analysts, including Lombard Odier's chief economist Samy Chaar and head of investment strategy Luca Bindelli.

—CNBC's Lim Hui Jie contributed to this report.

#2918. Fannie Mae, Freddie Mac rally as Ackman presses for conservatorship exit

The midterm elections could be a turning point for the release of Fannie Mae and Freddie Mac from government control, according to billionaire investor Bill Ackman.

Investors may agree with him, if the rally in both stocks is any sign.

The Federal National Mortgage Association, known as Fannie Mae, and Federal Home Loan Mortgage Corporation, or Freddie Mac, have each jumped more than 60% from Oct. 14 to Monday's close. The stocks were both up roughly 3% on Tuesday.

Ackman, the founder of Pershing Square Capital Management, has long pushed for the two entities to be released from federal conservatorship.

"The reason why it's not being touched right now is, you know, midterms," he said in an interview with CNBC's "Squawk Box."

Ackman, who owns both names through Pershing Square, believes Freddie and Fannie have the potential to increase "10-fold from current share prices," he said in his quarterly letter to shareholders, issued on Monday.

Fannie and Freddie buy mortgages from banks and other lenders, and then package them into mortgage-backed securities, known as MBS. Their operations are critical to the housing market, providing liquidity to banks and creating a secondary market for MBS.

The entities were placed into conservatorship in September 2008, during the financial crisis. The stocks were delisted from the New York Stock Exchange in 2010 and have traded over the counter since then.

Ackman wants the conservatorship to end, arguing the entities would be worth multiples of their current valuation if they were freed from government control.

President Donald Trump has supported ending the conservatorship of Fannie and Freddie. But it remains politically and economically contentious.

The entities operate under strict capital threshold requirements and would need to be recapitalized, which could happen through issuing common equity. However, the Treasury Department would need to figure out an exit plan for its roughly $388 billion ownership stake in the entities through preferred securities and warrants.

Congress may also need to play a role, passing legislation to restructure the government's role in mortgage financing and home-loan guarantees.

Nonetheless, Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent recently said it could happen before Trump's term ends, telling 2Way's "The Morning Meeting" podcast that the administration will tackle the ownership structure "next year, the year after." He said affordability will be guiding the decision.

One point of controversy is whether ending the conservatorship of Fannie and Freddie would increase the spreads on MBS over Treasuries, effectively making mortgages costlier. Bessent has sought to reassure the market that wouldn't happen.

"Whatever we do is going to be guided by making sure that the spread on [agency] mortgages … over the Treasury does not increase," Bessent said.

Ackman argues that privatizing the GSEs would help insulate them from political meddling. "Fannie and Freddie are these massive, enormously profitable institutions where whichever political party controls them…if they're still in conservatorship, can influence the outcome of elections," he told CNBC.

In one scenario, he explained, Democrats win the White House, conservatorship is still in place, and Democrats push Fannie and Freddie to lower credit standards in order to expand home ownership and affordability.

"If they're going to move more aggressively into borrowers with lower credit quality, for example, to achieve a political objective, that's going to have an impact on trillions of dollars outstanding mortgage securities," Ackman said. "It's going to have an impact on overall credit spreads."

MBS investors want certainty about how the entities will be run, he noted.

"That only gets resolved in a world in which they exit conservatorship, they become publicly traded companies that are owned by institutional and retail investors," Ackman said. "We set the rules. In conservatorship, the president can do anything."

#2917. Binance breach puts custody at center of crypto infrastructure trade

The $6.3 billion Binance hack could accelerate a shift in how investors value cryptocurrency companies, putting a greater premium on custody providers and the infrastructure needed to securely hold and protect digital assets.

Much of crypto's evolution this year has involved companies moving past their reliance on trading volumes and transaction fees, to focus instead on building the infrastructure connecting digital assets with traditional finance. Last Thursday's breach adds a new dimension, bringing cybersecurity, custody, compliance and balance-sheet resilience into sharper focus.

Importantly, the Binance breach was not an indictment of crypto itself, or a case of leadership-driven fraud, as with FTX in late 2022. Instead, Binance's leadership moved quickly to contain the fallout, protect customers and commit the exchange's own resources to cover losses.

On Tuesday, Binance CEO Richard Teng told CNBC's "Squawk on the Street" that the company has replenished 98% of $6.34 billion in affected assets, and that it is still in the process of purchasing outstanding, more illiquid assets.

Management's response, alongside efforts elsewhere in the industry to boost confidence, helped prevent a security breach from escalating into a broader crisis of confidence. For example, Tether, Circle, Ripple and others moved quickly to freeze or restrict access to about $3 billion in stolen assets; Coinbase blocked transfers to compromised wallets and offered Binance direct assistance.

The hack could even help shape the next phase of the crypto infrastructure trade. The new winners may not just be the businesses with the most users, volume or assets under custody, but the ones that can also demonstrate they're able to protect those assets under extreme duress.

"Which company has the resources to invest in cybersecurity and protect customer funds? For a smaller company, it could be much harder because once these crypto companies become infrastructure plays, providing the well for tokenization … they need to have the compliance and control in place so that a similar hacking will not happen or can be minimized," Owen Lau, senior analyst at ClearStreet, told CNBC. "Larger companies have more resources that could give them some advantage."

While institutional investors have increasingly embraced digital assets, broader adoption depends on confidence that those assets can be securely held, accessed and transferred. The Binance breach highlights the importance of that infrastructure, and could increase demand for specialized custodians and platforms with established asset-protection capabilities.

Coinbase has operated a dedicated institutional custody business since 2018. Circle entered the custody business this year, after the U.S. Office of the Comptroller of the Currency granted it approval to operate as a trust bank. In the past few years, Ripple and Kraken have also launched custody businesses.

Still, smaller players compete in the space as well. Small cap crypto exchange stock Gemini Space Station has offered custody services since 2019. BitGo Holdings, founded in 2013, is one of the earliest players in institutional crypto custody and infrastructure, with a reputation for security and serving major institutions.

The shift could also drive consolidation as companies look to buy capabilities, deepen their infrastructure offerings and achieve the scale needed to compete, Lau said.

The more consequential structural shift may be the separation of custody from execution, according to Morgan Stanley.

"Crypto exchanges have historically combined trading, custody, and settlement within vertically integrated platforms, concentrating operational risk. Repeated security breaches could encourage investors to move assets toward independent custody while continuing to trade on CEXs," the firm's analyst Felix Stratmann said in a note Monday," referring to centralized cryptocurrency exchanges.

"This would preserve the role of centralized exchanges as liquidity and execution venues, while potentially benefiting regulated custodians, including banks, as institutional investors place greater emphasis on asset segregation, operational resilience, and investor protection," Stratmann said.

#2915. The Golics Shows How ESPN Can Make Radio Content Work Well on Television

Between layoffs and general personnel changes, there seems to be a constant shift in the personalities we see across ESPN's multiple platforms. One unexpected move over the past year was forming The Golics, and the return of Mike Golic, who made a name for himself as one half of the Mike & Mike ESPN Radio show, which ran from 2000 to 2017.

On that show, Golic teamed with ESPN star Mike Greenberg in what became a historically popular national sports program. In his latest incarnation at ESPN, Golic teams with his son, Mike Golic Jr., on The Golics. An ESPN Radio show that also airs periodically on the network's television platforms.

I caught an episode this past week with two main goals.

First, I wanted to see if the senior Golic still had his sports talk fastball. Second, I wanted to see if this was a worthy vehicle for Golic Jr., who had previously bounced around in several roles at ESPN.

Both questions were answered with a resounding yes.

The show essentially started with a cold open featuring an extremely enthusiastic Golic Jr. rehashing the Kansas City Chiefs-Denver Broncos' tightly contested matchup from the previous weekend. The presentation is essentially a two-shot, with Golic Jr. on the left and his dad on the right.

There is a certain charm to this father-son combination. It brings a warm, family feel to the program.

On The Golics, highlights are interspersed with discussion. While graphics provide pertinent statistics on the topics being discussed. It was certainly nice to see and hear the elder Golic again. His return to the ESPN airwaves is most welcome after stops at both FanDuel and DraftKings.

Golic is an old-school, straight-up, hard-nosed analyst. The one who is going to tell you what he thinks, whether you like it or not. The father and son not only fit together as family members but also complement each other stylistically. Golic Jr. comes right at you with a powerful voice and equally strong opinions.

It is not all hardcore football talk with The Golics, either. Coming back from a break, they had some fun with a clip of LeBron James going into the crowd and taking a sip of a fan's wine before returning to the court. That led to a conversation about whether wine should even be served at a sporting event. This subject was right in the elder Golic's wheelhouse. On Mike & Mike, he was definitely the comedic wild card alongside Greenberg, the ultimate straight man.

The Golics also had a rather seriocomic discussion about the size and cost of muffins. Topics ventured into music and other areas, giving the show some variety.

However, what the Golics don't do is belabor the humor. They move through it quickly before bringing the conversation back to sports. This past week, the duo segued into their Golic Gamers awards, recognizing players who turned in outstanding performances over the weekend.

For me, the best part of the show is the forum it provides for Golic Jr. It is difficult to follow in the footsteps of an established sports media star who also happens to be your father. Just ask Jac Collinsworth, Joe Buck, Noah Eagle, the Caray family and many others.

Golic Jr. runs this show expertly, moving from topic to topic with verve and passion.

Golic Sr. offered some strong analysis of Gardner Minshew. He was coming off a tremendous game for the Cardinals last weekend, going 17-of-23 for 188 passing yards and three touchdowns in a comeback win over the Cowboys. Golic highlighted Minshew's performance, and his comments proved somewhat prophetic. The Cardinals subsequently handed Minshew the reins and agreed to trade former starter Jacoby Brissett to Baltimore.

While analyzing the Bears' 34-17 victory over the Seahawks on Monday Night Football, Golic Jr. said Chicago might be the best team in the NFC and was definitely the best team in the NFC North "with a bullet."

I like what the father and son bring to the table on this program. The connection they share as family members and former football players creates a more personal and engaging discourse when discussing sports.

ESPN, along with other networks, periodically airs many of its radio shows and podcasts on television. On these programs, visual variety is at a premium. Too often, the presentation consists of a single shot ad nauseam. On The Golics, graphics, highlights and audio clips help address that issue, providing much-needed visual breaks from the static two-shot.

When discussing the Baltimore Ravens' quarterback situation, Golic Jr. referenced facts that many viewers may not have known. Specifically, he pointed out that Lamar Jackson has considerable leverage in his contract situation because his deal precludes the Ravens from using either the franchise or transition tag on him.

Jackson also has a full no-trade clause and negotiates his own deals. It was a good tidbit from Golic Jr. that added concrete information to the speculation surrounding Jackson's future in Baltimore.

As the show closed, The Golics continued offering their views on several NFL teams. I particularly enjoyed their exchange about the Jacksonville Jaguars and why they deserve more attention. The Golics is an excellent combination of opinion and debate, effectively blending facts and fun with a unique family flair.

#2914. Democratic sweep puts crypto on defensive in Washington

Democrats' sweep of Congress likely resets the political calculus for crypto, ushering in a more adversarial approach in Washington and pushing industry-friendly legislation like the Clarity Act to the back burner.

With crypto critics like Sherrod Brown, D-Ohio, returning to the Senate and Sen. Elizabeth Warren, D-Mass., retaining significant influence over financial policy, analysts say the new Congress is likely to focus on scrutinizing President Donald Trump's personal crypto dealings over supportive industry legislation.

For years, crypto fought an uphill battle in Washington, asking for clear operating rules, but often landing on the receiving end of regulation by enforcement. That shifted after Trump's 2024 election victory, giving the industry a reprieve and seat at the policy table.

"We see Sherrod Brown winning in Ohio as damaging the crypto industry's political clout as the industry worked to defeat him," Jaret Seiberg, TD Cowen's Washington analyst, said in a note Wednesday. "We did not think Clarity could advance over the next two years. This makes us more confident in our prediction."

The crypto industry spent more than $200 million to sway the midterms, hoping to get leaders elected that could push a comprehensive crypto market structure bill over the finish line after the Clarity Act stalled in the Senate.

Seiberg emphasized that Democrats "will investigate Trump's crypto entanglements including World Liberty Financial" and said "the resulting political environment may make it impossible to advance a version of Clarity that the crypto sector could back."

Mike Townsend, Charles Schwab's managing director of legislative and regulatory affairs, said he expects "Democrats to push next year for a version of the bill that is much tougher on cryptocurrency companies."

The stakes extend beyond market structure legislation via Clarity. Stablecoin innovation; decentralized finance, or DeFi, development; banking access; tokenization; and sensible tax policy are priorities the industry sees as important to ensuring the U.S. remains competitive in the global crypto economy.

The industry will need continued bipartisan engagement to ensure these priorities aren't sidelined by changing political dynamics. And Democrats have historically been less supportive of crypto than Republicans, though that divide has narrowed as more Democrats embrace the industry.

The implications of Democrats' big win aren't all bad. Crypto has reached a level of financial integration that could be difficult to reverse, with its future increasingly centered on establishing crypto as financial infrastructure. Even with the Clarity Act potentially stalled until 2028, key financial regulators can help advance the industry through rule-making.

Coinbase's vice president of U.S. policy, Kara Calvert, points out that the election results point to a historic shift in the makeup of the incoming Congress, with a record number of pro-crypto voices expected to join its ranks.

"With last night's results, the 120th Congress is on track to have more new pro-crypto members than we've ever seen in any freshman class," she said. "Crypto is 100% bipartisan, and that's why the future of finance does not rest on either political party or a single election night. Democrats and Republicans alike know that clear rules protect consumers and keep innovation in the U.S."

As of Thursday morning, 19 candidates characterized as pro crypto by the advocacy nonprofit Stand With Crypto have been elected to the Senate versus eight Senate candidates it characterizes as anti crypto. In the House, 228 pro-crypto candidates have been elected versus 106 the group deems anti crypto.

Several races have not been called, but Fairshake — the super PAC launched in 2023 to support pro-crypto candidates regardless of party — has so far won 29 of the 34 races it spent on, per Reuters, including three of the six House candidates it prioritized by spending a baseline of $1 million on their campaigns.

Dante Disparte, chief strategy officer and head of global policy and operations at Circle, emphasized Circle's bipartisan approach and readiness for new Senate and House leadership, but said it "may take longer to get Clarity tabled again."

That bipartisan support may offer a blueprint for what comes next, but even if crypto legislation stalls, progress can continue, said Joseph Chalom, CEO of ether treasury company Sharplink.

"We expect the Clarity Act to remain on hold through 2028. Even so, there is a clear path forward for stablecoins … and for the tokenization of public equities under the SEC's five-year innovation exemption," he said. "Technological progress is rarely rolled back. Once the benefits of faster settlement, lower costs and markets that never close become clear to investors and institutions, this shift will be very difficult to reverse, regardless of who wins the White House in 2028."

Near-term, investors should expect more volatility as the market tries to figure out how crypto regulation may shift under a Democratic Congress.

The impact of the blue wave "should vary considerably across assets," said James Butterfill, head of research at CoinShares. "We would expect U.S. crypto equities to be more sensitive than bitcoin, particularly businesses reliant on new regulatory permissions. Established, compliant operators could even benefit from greater scrutiny of competitors. So miners who already have data centers set up are likely to remain in high demand by AI hyperscalers."

— CNBC's Talia Kaplan contributed reporting.

#2913. Polarizing NBA agitator Dillon Brooks sparks heated confrontation with Damian Lillard

Dillon Brooks literally — and physically — went there.

The known NBA antagonist appeared to stick his foot near the back of Damian Lillard's left Achilles, the same one he tore in the 2025 playoffs and sidelined him for all of the 2025-26 season, leading to a heated confrontation during the Trail Blazers' 132-119 home win over the Suns on Wednesday.

Lillard got right in Brooks' face and voiced his apparent displeasure before a referee broke up the mini fracas.

Phoenix Suns forward Dillon Brooks, left, and Portland Trail Blazers guard Damian Lillard talk during an NBA game, Wednesday, Nov. 4, 2026, in Phoenix. AP Photo/Rick Scuteri

The scene unfolded after Brooks stepped out of bounds with the ball in the third quarter while being guarded by Lillard, leading to the Suns wing hoisting a jumper that did not count.

Lillard stuck his hand up despite the whistle, and Brooks came down with his right leg extended.

It appeared his foot struck the lower part of Lillard's left leg, causing the Trail Blazers' veteran to immediately turn around and start jawing at Brooks.

Dillon Brooks' foot appears to connect with the back of Damian Lillard's left leg. @CGBBurner/X

#2912. CNBC's experts on whether the AI trade is ending and what investors should do next

Amid a powerful rout in tech stocks, investors told CNBC on Thursday that something may be fundamentally breaking in the AI trade following the big success of Democrats in Tuesday's midterm elections and the threat they try to slow the data center buildout.

Democrats are more aggressively anti-data center than Republicans, and Wall Street is quickly redoing its math what any restrictions they implement could mean for the related companies' future growth.

"Right now, investors are questioning that $1.2 trillion of capex that we forecast for next year," Peter Boockvar, chief investment officer at One Point BFG Wealth Partners, told CNBC. "Well, maybe that's not going to happen."

Boockvar believes this could be the start of the end of the AI trade as we know it.

Whether it's completely over or not, investors need to be reassessing how they're planning to play the AI trade going forward, the analysts said.

The Nasdaq Composite dropped 5% on Thursday and fell into correction territory, off by more than 11% from its record high set last month. The iShares Semiconductor ETF dropped 11% on Thursday, its biggest decline since during the Covid breakout in March 2020. The Global X Artificial Intelligence & Technology ETF (AIQ) is down 6% on the day and down 15% over the past month.

"There has been a huge change in the outlook for AI infrastructure buildout," Eric Johnston, head of equities at Cantor Fitzgerald, told CNBC. "The entire economy and the consumer and earnings growth are all levered to that buildout."

The selling Thursday spilled over to non-AI stocks with the Dow Jones Industrial average down more than 1,600 points.

Josh Brown pointed to the 4.8% yield for the 10-year Treasury yield, saying a compelling risk-free rate is causing investors to reconsider taking the risk in these volatile AI stocks.

"You don't need to be at 80% U.S. stocks and have 60% of that exposure be tech," Brown said.

Many investors believe the AI trade isn't over completely, investors just need to get more selective.

"The AI buildout just got harder," said Rob Sechan, Newedge Capital Group Founder & Managing Partner:

"Nobody voted to make compute less scarce," Sechan said, citing Microsoft's Azure cloud business growing 48% year over year in its most recent third quarter. That level of growth suggests its capacity constrained, not demand constrained, he said.

"When new capacity gets harder to build, existing capacity becomes more valuable," Sechan said.

Richard Clode, portfolio manager at Janus Henderson, noted a few bright spots in tech on Thursday despite the highly correlated downward movements in the big tech indices.

"Motorola Solutions had some good results," he said. "We're seeing a little bit of rotation into some of the … internet names...Sony reported some good results, and again they were up strongly overnight."

"So there are some brief pockets of respite from this, but obviously pretty painful across the board today," he said.

Some experts believe the selling was overdone and investors could start nibbling.

This "indiscriminate selling" is a "buying opportunity," said Larry Adam, Raymond James chief investment officer. If investors "take a longer term perspective, which is what we do, I think the valuations at today's levels will tend to prove to be a good buying opportunity," he said, urging investors to look at the fundamentals.

Victoria Greene, chief investment officer of G Squared Private Wealth, said she saw some strong supports around the S&P 500's 200-day moving average and was on the lookout for a market bounce following Thursday's rout.

"Typically, the average return is plus about 180 basis points the next day," she said. "The question we have to ask ourselves: Is this a pullback in an uptrend, which is what I believe? Or is this the end-all, be-all [and] the wheels are coming off and everybody just needs to buy T-bills? I don't think we're there yet."

#2911. Alex Highsmith reveals what has helped the Steelers’ defense play at a high level

The Pittsburgh Steelers defense held the Cleveland Browns to season lows in points and rushing yards in their dominant 27-6 victory at Acrisure Stadium this past Sunday. As they enter the bye, the Steelers defense ranks third in scoring defense, allowing just 18 points per game through eight games. However, it wasn't always sunshine and rainbows for the defensive unit early in the season.

In fact, the first time the Steelers and Browns met this season, T.J. Watt was caught yelling into a headset on the sideline at defense coordinator Patrick Graham after a miscommunication led to a Cleveland touchdown. After that game, Graham moved from the booth to the sidelines with his players. The Steelers have rattled off four consecutive wins since Graham moved to the sidelines, backed by exceptional defensive play.

Appearing on "NFL Report" with Andrew Siciliano, Steelers edge rusher Alex Highsmith detailed how Graham's move down to the sidelines has changed the Steelers' defense.

"You know, I do believe that was a turning point for us," Highsmith said. "Obviously, he's been on the field with us ever since and so just him being down there is just really helped us a lot, for sure. But, you know PG's been awesome, ever since he came in he's just had a desire for us to be great. He's a football guy, he knows football so well, and so I just think as we continue to gel as a unit, gel as a defense, you know it's only going to be better from here on out. Like I said, I just love the way we are progressing as a defense so we just gotta continue to play like we have been and with him (Graham) calling plays, just excited to see what he continues to scheme up to put guys in positions to make plays."

Pittsburgh boasts the third most takeaways in the league with 13, while allowing just 83.9 rushing yards per game to opponents at the midway point in their season. It seems like Pittsburgh finally has the right man leading the charge defensively in Graham. Highsmith's praise of the scheme and putting guys in positions to make plays is a great sign that the players have bought into what Graham is selling them. The Steelers look well on their way to having their first season with a top-ten scoring defense since 2023, and it seems like it only took a simple move to the sidelines to make a world of difference.

#2910. Yankees Trade Pitch Lands Jazz Chisholm Replacement in 4-For-1 White Sox Deal

Chase Meidroth #10 of the Chicago White Sox would go to the Yankees in a new trade pitch. Getty

Anthony Volpe had to Chicago under the framework of a trade pitch published Thursday. Getty

Jazz Chisholm Jr. is headed for free agency without a qualifying offer from the New York Yankees, and a trade pitch published Thursday by Baseball Trade Values and aimed at the Yankees would replace him with a former Boston Red Sox prospect.

The Yankees' $23.15 million qualifying offer to David Bednar came Monday, but none went to Chisholm, a strong indication that the Yankees are fully prepared to let Chisholm walk in free agency, opening a second base vacancy. But would White Sox second baseman Chase Meidroth be an upgrade?

The site, which grades proposed deals with numerical values assigned to each player, would send Anthony Volpe, Elmer Rodriguez, Will Warren and Ben Hess to the Chicago White Sox, valuing that package at 53.4 in surplus against 42.2 for Meidroth.

GettyChase Meidroth #10 of the Chicago White Sox would go to the Yankees in a new trade pitch.

Chisholm, 28, a two-time All-Star born in Nassau, Bahamas, arrived from Miami in July 2024 for catcher Agustín Ramírez and two infielders. He hit .231/.312/.399 with 19 homers and a career-high 41 steals this season, but struck out in 29.5% of his plate appearances. His glove graded at plus-15 Outs Above Average and minus-6 Defensive Runs Saved.

A year earlier, Chisholm slashed .242/.332/.481 with 31 homers. He then went 2-for-11 against Tampa Bay in the division series, made a throwing error in Game 2 and sat two games.

"This is where I want to be," Chisholm said after the sweep, as quoted by the New York Post. Former MLB general manager Jim Bowden was less sure, saying on "Foul Territory" he would "probably pass on both" Chisholm and Ketel Marte if he were still running a baseball organization.

Chisholm earned $10.2 million this season. He reportedly sought $35 million annually over eight to 10 years, while an executive projected three or four years near $20 million, according to MLB.com. Meidroth earned $789,100 last season, and is not eligible for arbitration until 2028.

GettyAnthony Volpe had to Chicago under the framework of a trade pitch published Thursday.

Meidroth, 25, a Torrance, California, native and University of San Diego product, was a Red Sox fourth-round pick in 2022 who arrived at the White Sox in the Garrett Crochet trade, and hit .276/.350/.415 with 16 homers in 2026. His .765 OPS topped Chisholm's .711, and his 4.5 fWAR led American League second basemen, a full win above Chisholm's 3.5.

Chisholm owns the edge in power and speed.

The price is four players. Rodriguez, 23, from Trujillo Alto, Puerto Rico, is the No. 2 prospect in the system and went 0-3 with a 5.40 ERA in six starts, striking out 21 over 26⅔ innings. The Red Sox drafted him in the fourth round in 2021, and he came from Boston for Carlos Narvaez.

Volpe, 25, a Manhattan native and 2019 first-rounder, won the 2023 AL Gold Glove at shortstop. Volpe hit .212/.272/.391 in 2025 and returned from shoulder surgery to bat .245 with two homers in 2026, after signing a one-year, $3.48 million contract.

Warren, 27, a Brandon, Mississippi, native and eighth-round pick out of Southeastern Louisiana, went 11-6 with a 3.92 ERA and 158 strikeouts in 156 innings.

Hess, 24, from Charleston, Illinois, was the 26th overall pick in 2024 out of Alabama and posted a 4.45 ERA over 56⅔ innings before the 60-day injured list ended his year, after a 3.22 ERA across 103⅓ innings in 2025.

Competitively, Chicago gets a starter, a former Gold Glove shortstop and two pitching prospects, while New York fills second base but loses Warren's 29 starts. By the site's model, Chicago wins by 11.2 points, and Warren alone makes up more than half of Meidroth's 42.2. The two clubs rarely deal. A seven-player trade in July 2017 brought Todd Frazier, David Robertson and Tommy Kahnle to New York. The latest sent reliever Keynan Middleton to New York for Juan Carela on Aug. 1, 2023.

#2905. Elder's Kaden Estep is rewriting Panther records in senior season

Nearly 40 years ago, Steve Keller dominated the Elder Panther quarterback records. The starting quarterback in 1987 and 1988, Keller amassed 4,816 yards and 45 touchdowns in his career.

Twenty years after Keller, Mark Miller led Elder to the Division I state championship game in 2008 and the semifinals in 2009. Along the way, he broke both of Keller's career records with 5,872 yards and 56 touchdowns.

Miller's touchdown record still stands, but his yardage record didn't get that 20-year cushion. First, Peyton Ramsey broke it with 6,708 yards (and took second with 49 touchdowns) from 2013 to 2015, but the Elder record books were rewritten again in 2026 and the pencil is still sharpened to take down another.

Elder senior quarterback and Cincinnati Bearcats commit Kaden Estep broke Ramsey's record with a 13-yard pass to Jake Bier in a 62-6 win over Mount Zion Prep on Oct. 23. Estep finished the game with 6,861 career passing yards. His five touchdown passes in that game put him at 53 for his career, just three behind Miller.

"Earned," Ramsey, now an assistant coach at Elder, posted on X after Estep's record-breaking night. "The greatest QB to ever put on the uniform. Period. An absolute winner. All of it a byproduct of the type of teammate he is. Keep going!"

Since Estep took over as a sophomore in 2024, Elder has gone 26-9, including an undefeated 2025 regular season and Greater Catholic-League South championship. It was Elder's first GCL-South crown since Ramsey led Elder to a split with St. Xavier in 2015 and first solo title since Miller led the Panthers in '08.

Miller, now a teacher at Taylor, was previously the head coach for the Yellowjackets after coaching junior varsity at Elder. Seeing Ramsey initially break his yardage record was a full-circle moment.

"I remember Peyton and I remember one of (assistant coach) Craig James' sons, Jakob, being ball boys and running around," Miller said. "Your typical coach's kid that Peyton is has been living, breathing and eating Elder football since the time he could walk, I'm sure.

"It's been fun to watch to kind of watch him progress from the player that I got to watch after I graduated to the coach and the teacher that he is now."

Miller got an early look at what Coach Peyton Ramsey would look like when Ramsey was just starting his college career at Indiana.

"I texted him," Miller said. "I was like, 'Hey, I need your help on drawing some of these plays up because I've been removed far enough now where all this language is new.' When I asked (Elder head coach) Doug (Ramsey), I said, 'Doug, do you have a playbook for me?' And I'll never forget it; he literally pointed to his head and that was it. I was like, 'Well, I'm going to need some help from the guy who was just here.'

"Even then and obviously as a player, he was special and could break things down and do things a lot better than other players in the past have. Just seeing how cerebral he was at the time as a 19-, 20-year-old kid, you know he could have coached that offense while he was playing and certainly while he was in college."

Ramsey is in his sixth season as an assistant for his dad. He and Estep are the only two quarterbacks to be three-year starters in Doug's 30 seasons as Elder head coach.

The younger Ramsey ‒ who still clears all other Elder QBs with 9,400 total yards of offense, according to Elder statistician Kelby Siler ‒ has worked closely with Estep to turn him from a good sophomore to an all-time great high school quarterback.

Estep had 1,908 yards and 12 touchdowns to eight interceptions as a sophomore in 2024 when Elder went 6-6. He also ran for 189 yards on 91 carries with two touchdowns and six fumbles.

The next season, Estep led Elder to a 12-1 record and a trip to the regional finals. He threw for 2,430 yards and 15 touchdowns and ran for 924 more yards and 10 touchdowns on 131 carries.

Estep's senior season, with an upcoming playoff game against Springboro, has seen him flip a Miami RedHawks commitment to a Cincinnati Bearcats commitment. The Bearcat offer came two days after a loss to St. Xavier, where he completed 20-of-26 passes for 236 yards and three touchdowns, with 72 rushing yards and another score.

In 10 games in 2026, Estep has completed 164 of 223 passes for 2,523 yards and 26 touchdowns, with just three picks. He has run for 780 yards and eight touchdowns.

Estep needs three touchdowns to tie Miller for the career mark, a number Estep has done eight times in his career, including five times this season.

#2903. LeBron James' lawyers send cease and desist to AI sports-betting app that used his name and likeness

Attorneys for Philadelphia 76ers star LeBron James sent a cease and desist letter to an AI sports-betting app that used a popular nickname for James and released an ad featuring his likeness, according to Front Office Sports.

Bron AI, a sports-gambling app, put out the ad Wednesday. In it, an AI version of James can be seen placing bets on NBA outcomes.

The ad, which has already been taken down on X, never showed James' face in AI, but showed a basketball player who looked a lot like James from behind. The player in the ad was wearing a No. 23 jersey that read "Bron" on the back, making it an exceptionally obvious reference to James.

In the cease and desist letter, which was obtained by Front Office Sports, James' attorneys make it clear James "neither approved any use and/or exploitation of, nor authorized or granted permission" to his name or likeness being used in the ad.

Additionally, James' attorneys pointed out that the 76ers star already has an "exclusive endorsement arrangement" with Polymarket and that the Bron AI ad appeared to show James' character placing bets on NBA games, which is "a very serious violation of NBA rules and regulations."

James is, apparently, not the only NBA star to issue a cease and desist letter to the company. James' former teammate, Los Angeles Lakers star Luka Dončić, reportedly also asked the company to stop using his likeness. Dončić's picture was used in the ad. Pictures of other athletes, such as Giannis Antetokounmpo, Jayden Daniels, Christian McCaffrey and Donovan Mitchell also appeared in the ad.

Given the egregious nature of the violation, Bron AI quickly pulled the ad from social media. It's unclear whether the company will face further backlash for the stunt.

#2901. Thursday Night Football: DE Travon Walker, RB Bhayshul Tuten active for Jaguars vs. Ravens

#2899. Elder's Kaden Estep is rewriting Panther records in senior season

Nearly 40 years ago, Steve Keller dominated the Elder Panther quarterback records. The starting quarterback in 1987 and 1988, Keller amassed 4,816 yards and 45 touchdowns in his career.

Twenty years after Keller, Mark Miller led Elder to the Division I state championship game in 2008 and the semifinals in 2009. Along the way, he broke both of Keller's career records with 5,872 yards and 56 touchdowns.

Miller's touchdown record still stands, but his yardage record didn't get that 20-year cushion. First, Peyton Ramsey broke it with 6,708 yards (and took second with 49 touchdowns) from 2013 to 2015, but the Elder record books were rewritten again in 2026 and the pencil is still sharpened to take down another.

Elder senior quarterback and Cincinnati Bearcats commit Kaden Estep broke Ramsey's record with a 13-yard pass to Jake Bier in a 62-6 win over Mount Zion Prep on Oct. 23. Estep finished the game with 6,861 career passing yards. His five touchdown passes in that game put him at 53 for his career, just three behind Miller.

"Earned," Ramsey, now an assistant coach at Elder, posted on X after Estep's record-breaking night. "The greatest QB to ever put on the uniform. Period. An absolute winner. All of it a byproduct of the type of teammate he is. Keep going!"

Since Estep took over as a sophomore in 2024, Elder has gone 26-9, including an undefeated 2025 regular season and Greater Catholic-League South championship. It was Elder's first GCL-South crown since Ramsey led Elder to a split with St. Xavier in 2015 and first solo title since Miller led the Panthers in '08.

Miller, now a teacher at Taylor, was previously the head coach for the Yellowjackets after coaching junior varsity at Elder. Seeing Ramsey initially break his yardage record was a full-circle moment.

"I remember Peyton and I remember one of (assistant coach) Craig James' sons, Jakob, being ball boys and running around," Miller said. "Your typical coach's kid that Peyton is has been living, breathing and eating Elder football since the time he could walk, I'm sure.

"It's been fun to watch to kind of watch him progress from the player that I got to watch after I graduated to the coach and the teacher that he is now."

Miller got an early look at what Coach Peyton Ramsey would look like when Ramsey was just starting his college career at Indiana.

"I texted him," Miller said. "I was like, 'Hey, I need your help on drawing some of these plays up because I've been removed far enough now where all this language is new.' When I asked (Elder head coach) Doug (Ramsey), I said, 'Doug, do you have a playbook for me?' And I'll never forget it; he literally pointed to his head and that was it. I was like, 'Well, I'm going to need some help from the guy who was just here.'

"Even then and obviously as a player, he was special and could break things down and do things a lot better than other players in the past have. Just seeing how cerebral he was at the time as a 19-, 20-year-old kid, you know he could have coached that offense while he was playing and certainly while he was in college."

Ramsey is in his sixth season as an assistant for his dad. He and Estep are the only two quarterbacks to be three-year starters in Doug's 30 seasons as Elder head coach.

The younger Ramsey ‒ who still clears all other Elder QBs with 9,400 total yards of offense, according to Elder statistician Kelby Siler ‒ has worked closely with Estep to turn him from a good sophomore to an all-time great high school quarterback.

Estep had 1,908 yards and 12 touchdowns to eight interceptions as a sophomore in 2024 when Elder went 6-6. He also ran for 189 yards on 91 carries with two touchdowns and six fumbles.

The next season, Estep led Elder to a 12-1 record and a trip to the regional finals. He threw for 2,430 yards and 15 touchdowns and ran for 924 more yards and 10 touchdowns on 131 carries.

Estep's senior season, with an upcoming playoff game against Springboro, has seen him flip a Miami RedHawks commitment to a Cincinnati Bearcats commitment. The Bearcat offer came two days after a loss to St. Xavier, where he completed 20-of-26 passes for 236 yards and three touchdowns, with 72 rushing yards and another score.

In 10 games in 2026, Estep has completed 164 of 223 passes for 2,523 yards and 26 touchdowns, with just three picks. He has run for 780 yards and eight touchdowns.

Estep needs three touchdowns to tie Miller for the career mark, a number Estep has done eight times in his career, including five times this season.

This article originally appeared on Cincinnati Enquirer: Elder's Kaden Estep is rewriting Panther records in senior season

#2898. Lakers’ Walker Kessler surprised where he ranks in key statistical category

SACRAMENTO — Walker Kessler has been every bit of the rim protector the Lakers hoped he would be when they made the investment they did to acquire him from the Jazz.

Lakers center Walker Kessler (14) has once again been one of the league's best rim protectors. AP Photo/Bethany Baker

Opponents shoot significantly worse and less near the rim when the 7-foot-2 center is on the floor.

He's top seven in the league in defensive field-goal percentage near the rim.

And after blocking four shots during the Lakers' 116-103 win over the Kings on Wednesday, Kessler ranked third in blocked shots per game entering Thursday — a stat that surprised him.

Walker Kessler was surprised when he found out he didn't rank second in blocked shots per game. Getty Images

"Third? I know [Victor Wembanyama's] ahead of me," Kessler said. "Who else is ahead of me?"

Kessler added: "But it's crazy. I thought I was at least No. 2 That's crazy."

Wembanyama, the reigning Defensive Player of the Year, was an easy guess considering he leads the league at 4.5 blocked shots per game after blocking eight shots in the Spurs' win over the Mavericks on Wednesday.

The only other player averaging more blocked shots than Kessler? Bucks big man Myles Turner, who entered Thursday averaging 2.8 blocked shots per game compared with Kessler's 2.7.

Walker Kessler has blocked the second-most shots so far this season. AP Photo/Mark J. Terrill

"Really?" Kessler responded when this information was relayed to him.

Technically, Kessler has blocked more shots this season. He's recorded 24 blocked shots, while Turner has 22.

But Kessler's nine games played compared with Turner's eight gives the Bucks center the edge when it comes to the season-long average.

It's unlikely either will threaten Wembanyama when it comes to the most blocked shots per game this season. The Spurs 7-4 phenom has led the NBA in the statistical category each season since being the top draft pick in 2023. But the race for No. 2 will be a fun one to monitor.

#2893. Broncos pass rush has returned with a fury through eight games

While the sack numbers are a bit off pace to the previous two seasons, the pressure rate is actually higher. The Denver Broncos defense is forcing quarterbacks to get rid of the ball quick or face the consequences.

144 quarterback pressures. 12 more than second place.

74 combined sacks and QB hits. 15 more than second place.

51 quarterback hits. 11 more than second place.

PFF's top ranked pass rush grade. Best pass rush win rate.

The reason the sacks are off pace is due to the competition this defense has faced. These quarterbacks are really good at avoiding sacks: Patrick Mahomes x2, Trevor Lawrence, Matthew Stafford, and Brock Purdy. Those guys have a combined 41 sacks between them. The other three quarterbacks Denver faced: Justin Herbert, Jacoby Brisset, and Sam Darnold have a combined 59 sacks between them.

Of the remaining eight quarterbacks on the schedule, four quarterbacks are in the Top 5 in sacks taken and all but Josh Allen and Kirk Cousins are in the Top 10. Those sack numbers should start to stack for this Broncos defense down the stretch.

Broncos focused on taking care of business against Panthers

The Denver Broncos have a shot to be 7-2 before their bye week if they can topple the Carolina Panthers on Sunday.

How the Denver Broncos can beat the Carolina Panthers

The Denver Broncos head on the road to face the Carolina Panthers in Week 9. Here is how they can earn their seventh win of the season.

Broncos vs. Panthers: 3 Things to Watch in Week 9

Here are three storylines to watch when the Denver Broncos battle the Carolina Panthers on Sunday.

Alex Singleton named AFC Defensive Player of the Month

What a turnaround from Week 1 for Alex Singleton and the entire defense. Singleton especially has been balling out during the Denver Broncos four game win streak!

On the defense's performance in goal-line situations

"I think we just play. We don't worry about where the ball's put down. We just play. Those things we practice every single week. I'm not surprised that we're good down there. We spent a bunch of time down there. It's been our fourth year together. We have our four or five calls we love down there that we're good at. So if we do it right, we expect to win."

On if he's surprised that ILB Alex Singleton wasn't previously recognized for his high level of play

"No, that's the league. When you're playing well on defense, guys do get recognized. He's been consistent for a long time. He had 175 tackles [three] years ago. No one talks about that. I'm not surprised he's playing well again."

On young players like DE Sai'vion Jones and OLB Que Robinson being ready to play

"It's coaching. I think when you're picking your roster, you have to pick your roster with guys that fit your scheme. I think here that's what we've done. So when you lose one player, we have guys who can fill in who have the same skill set. So I'm not surprised those kids play well. Again it's NFL football. It's the same coach, it's the same room. When it's their turn to play, they play to the standard. If they don't fit and they can't play, they shouldn't be here. Every guy on this team who makes our roster on defense, if they have to play for us, nothing changes in my mind. Let's go win the game. There are certain areas where I can't call certain things with certain players, but the standard of doing it right, that never changes here."

On determining special teams rotations during games

"I think one of the things people might not recognize is we do a pretty good job of keeping track of reps during the game. We have guys up in the box kind of doing a rep count and making sure no one's getting too many. As you know, there are some games where you might have 18 special teams plays, and there are some games where you might have 35. It really just depends how the game plays out. So we'll do a good job. We'll have a plan kind of going in. Dispel those guys if needed. They're going to have to do obviously some things. They might not do the full workload as they normally do. Then again, they might depending on how the game plays out. So we'll kind of track that as we go. Yes, my depth chart sheet will be an interesting one. Be like the old, what's the movie? The old Matrix. It'll be a lot of stuff going on there."

On the challenges of kicking field goals and extra points in the rain and how they prepare for those conditions

"I think obviously because we play outdoors at our home games, weekly we'll maybe do some wet ball drills and things like that. It's weird when the K-balls are a different ball than the quarterback ball. They're a little bit smoother because they get kind of worked. So when those balls get a little bit wet, they actually get really slippery. It's not the ball that's soaked, it's the ball when it just starts raining. If you've talked to all the specialists over the years, the snappers and the holders and the punter catching the operation, those are the ones. So right away when you see any precipitation come down, those guys are off on the side and they're getting work and whether or not they use a towel, gloves, whatever we're allowed to use. Yes, it certainly makes that operation. So as soon as we know we're going to get any kind of weather, we certainly practice it. Then those guys on gameday are going to be working on the sideline and gauging the weather."

On the challenges of kicking at sea level when they're accustomed to altitude

"I think the pregame warmup for us may be more important than any other position because you kind of get an idea of how the ball's going to travel on that particular day, whether it's wind, weather. I've coached at sea level for more than 16 years in the NFL and then a bunch of years in college. The ball may not travel as well. We'll see what the weather is there. The pregame for the kicker and the punter is probably more important than people would anticipate because you kind of get the wind direction, you know which way if it's sideways wind or north-south wind, if you will. Then how well the ball's traveling. So we kind of have, [Head] Coach [Sean Payton] will always ask me right before kickoff, 'What's our line going each way for us and them?' So the ball certainly travels a little bit better here in altitude, but I think sometimes it gets overblown, overdid a little bit. We'll see how it is there in pregame on Sunday morning."

On the spark RB Jonah Coleman brings to the offense

"I think that started since rookie minicamp, and then the preseason game against Atlanta, and then the Jacksonville game and now this game. I think every time he gets an opportunity, he's showed up. His teammates love him. He's smart, he's mature and he's going to continue to get better, which is awesome."

On how Head Coach Sean Payton has prepared the team for its next game following an emotional win

"I think if you look at Carolina's record, you can be one of those guys that overlooks them. If you look at the Chicago game, they put up a lot of points on offense. You look at the Detroit game, they beat them. There are plenty of examples of this team being really, really good there. A playoff team last year, arguably should have beat the Rams in the playoffs last year. So they're a couple plays away from having a winning record. This is the NFL. There are not many cupcakes out there, so this is going to be a good one on Sunday."

On the challenge of facing a team with a new defensive coordinator

"I think the first quarter's going to be interesting in regards to seeing what they have new, what's kind of the same. [They have] a couple of players that are kind of banged up. Are they going to play or are they not? Obviously schematics. So I think the first quarter's going to be a really interesting quarter. Then I think the game's going to settle down within that second to the fourth. You could say the same thing about Kansas City versus us. Obviously it's still [Head Coach] Sean [Payton]'s offense. There wasn't much changed. Midseason changing a bunch is probably going to be tough, but there'll definitely be some wrinkles and we're prepared to attack it."
