#2884. Thunder’s Latest Signing Reveals Unexpected Alex Caruso Connection

PHILADELPHIA, PENNSYLVANIA - MARCH 10: Dalen Terry #14 of the Philadelphia 76ers dribbles the ball during a game against the Memphis Grizzlies at Xfinity Mobile Arena on March 10, 2026 in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Emilee Chinn/Getty Images) Getty

The Oklahoma City Thunder may have uncovered more than additional roster depth with their latest signing. General manager Sam Presti brought Dalen Terry to Oklahoma City, reuniting the former Chicago Bulls first-round pick with someone who helped shape his NBA career.

That player is Alex Caruso, one of the Thunder's most valuable defensive veterans. Terry recently revealed that Caruso was more than a teammate during their time together in Chicago. He was a mentor whose playing style became a blueprint for Terry's development.

"I always thought my niche in the league could be like Alex Caruso," Terry said in comments shared by Full Court Press. "And even though we're a little different, I just think that I could see him and I could see myself."

Terry explained that Caruso regularly helped him understand defensive responsibilities and prepare for the demands of an NBA season. That relationship began early in Terry's career, when both players were members of the Bulls.

"So it was always easy to ask him in the off-seasons what he did to prepare for the seasons and even in the game, different coverages and stuff," Terry said.

The comparison becomes particularly interesting when examining their career production per 36 minutes, a statistic that estimates what players might produce with equal playing time.

GettyPHILADELPHIA, PENNSYLVANIA – MARCH 10: Dalen Terry #14 of the Philadelphia 76ers dribbles the ball during a game against the Memphis Grizzlies at Xfinity Mobile Arena on March 10, 2026 in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Emilee Chinn/Getty Images)

Caruso has averaged approximately 11.2 points, 4.8 rebounds, 4.5 assists and 2.2 steals per 36 minutes. Terry's corresponding figures are 11.3 points, 5.3 rebounds, 3.9 assists and 1.6 steals.

Those similarities deserve attention, although they do not establish that Terry offers the same impact. Caruso has built his reputation through elite defensive anticipation, positioning and performance in meaningful games. Terry still must demonstrate that his production translates into a consistent role.

Nevertheless, Terry's rebounding, passing and defensive activity suggest there is something worth developing.

Oklahoma City officially announced Terry's multiyear signing Wednesday, adding a 24-year-old wing with 218 games of NBA experience. Injuries to several important rotation players have increased the Thunder's need for additional depth.

What makes Terry particularly intriguing is his familiarity with the defensive philosophy Caruso represents. Oklahoma City prioritizes versatility, communication and players willing to contribute without dominating possessions.

Caruso's mentorship could help Terry adapt to those expectations.

"And A.C.'s always been a coach on the court," Terry said. "Even my rookie year and my second year when he was there, he always helped me. So I always appreciate him for that."

Terry is not another Caruso simply because their statistics resemble one another. However, learning directly from an established defensive specialist gives him a meaningful foundation.

For Presti and the Thunder, that connection could make this signing considerably more valuable than an ordinary depth addition.

#2883. Fantasy Football 2026: Week 9 preview — Start/sit and more

Welcome to Week 9. Somehow, we are already at the halfway point of the 18-week NFL season. That was fast.

Week 8 was strange. Tampa beat Atlanta 16-0 without completing a single pass. Dallas and Kansas City both blew double-digit fourth quarter leads. Joe Burrow accounted for six total touchdowns. Running backs absolutely ran wild on Sunday. Baltimore lost two quarterbacks to what might be season-ending injuries in the first half at Buffalo. Minnesota held Detroit to its lowest point total of the season, but also scored the fewest against them, and absorbed its first loss. The Giants grew their NFC East lead without playing. It was that kind of weekend – eerie and weird — kind of fitting for Halloween.

What's in store this week? Six teams – Philadelphia, Seattle, Baltimore, Minnesota, fCleveland, and Arizona – are switching to a different starting quarterback. That raises a bunch of questions and decisions for fantasy managers.

Stats of the Week, Bucs-Falcons (sorry, this HAS TO get its own section – one day you'll tell your grandkids about that time in 2026 when two NFL teams played a high school football game):

Tampa became the first team since 1974, and the third since the forward pass was invented in the 1930s, to win a game without completing a single pass.

Tampa is the first team in NFL history to have two players (Bucky Irving and Kenneth Gainwell) with 25+ rushes and 125+ rushing yards in the same game.

Tampa's defenders caught four more passes than Tampa's offensive players.

Despite not completing a pass, and rushing for just 14 yards with no TDs, Baker Mayfield had more fantasy points than, and triple the passer rating of, Michael Penix, Jr.

Over their past two games (both wins, with 59 total points scored), the Bucs called 16 passes and 104 runs, and Baker Mayfield had seven completions for 64 yards.

No team in AFC North is over .500. Every team in the NFC North is over .500. Every team in the NFC South is under .500.

The NFC North is a combined 22-3 in non-divisional games.

Bo Nix has engineered 13 fourth quarter comebacks – the most in NFL history for a quarterback in his first three seasons.

Since the start of last season, the Broncos are 3-3 in games where they trailed by 11+ points during the fourth quarter. The rest of the NFL is 11-215 in such situations.

For the first time since 2003, six running backs had at least 140 rushing yards on Sunday.

The Browns have lost 23 straight games in Pittsburgh, which ties the NFL record for a road losing streak vs. one team.

The Bears have 17 rushing touchdowns, which is the most for a team through their first eight games since 1956.

Since 2000, Arizona was 1-147-1 when trailing by 14 points or more in the fourth quarter, and Dallas was 69-0 at home when leading by 14 points or more in the fourth quarter. That all changed on Sunday with the Cardinals' late comeback.

On to Week 9. Note: CIN @ ATL is being played in Spain. Set those lineups early!

For those who are familiar with this column, you know the drill. For everyone else: The Rides, Fades, and Sleepers is an analysis of players that I think are primed for an especially strong or poor performance, often as compared to consensus expectations. This isn't a straight-up Start/Sit exercise. As a general rule, start your studs. I'll rarely list the most obvious names at a position as "Rides" because those players are almost always expected to have strong performances. You don't need me or anyone else telling you to start Joe Burrow. While this analysis is intended for season-long play, it works for DFS formats too. Half PPR scoring and Expert Consensus Rankings (ECR) are used for the column.

Each week, I'm picking a Ride, Fade, and Sleeper of the Week. The rules: The Ride of the Week can't be a truly elite option, the Fade can't be someone who nobody is starting anyway, and the Sleeper must be an actual sleeper, from down in the rankings.

Last week, my Rides, Fades, and Sleepers did well, with a lot more hits than misses. You can check my work here.

My biggest miss was my Ride of the Week, Zay Flowers. He scored zero points. I'll repeat that – A bagel. A donut. Zero point zero. I obviously couldn't foresee the quarterback injuries, but I'll take the L. My Fade of the Week hit (Bryce Young, 11.4 fantasy points, QB18) and so did my Sleeper of the Week (Geno Smith, 19 fantasy points, QB13).

Ride of the Week: Matthew Stafford (@WAS). Stafford is ranked outside the Top-10 QBs this week and I think that's too low. The Commanders can score behind Jayden Daniels and they also give up points. This game's Vegas total of 50.5 is the second highest on the board and I think it goes over. Washington is tough to run on, but generous in the passing game. They're one of seven teams that has allowed at least 15 TD passes so far this season, and they've given up the third most fantasy points per game (FPPG) to opposing QBs. Stafford is coming off his second worst fantasy output of the season and I think he'll rebound. He came out of last week's game with a sore elbow, so keep an eye on that.

Fade of the Week: Zay Flowers (vs. JAC). Well, this is a first for me, as Flowers goes from Ride of the Week to Fade of the Week in just five days. UDFA rookie Joe Fagnano gets his first NFL start for the Ravens, on a short week and a bad ankle. If the Ravens get to ten points, I'll be surprised. Their implied total is 15, which is the second lowest on the Board, and they haven't topped 13 points in any of their last three games. Jacoby Brissett will help – in Week 10. Jacksonville's defense must be licking its chops after it throttled another dead team last week. Flowers is a terrific receiver who was having a great season before last Sunday, but this QB situation is too much to overcome. He's ranked inside the Top-20 at the position this week and I have him outside my Top-30.

Sleeper of the Week: Drew Lock (vs. ARI). The Cardinals are coming off that improbable comeback in Dallas, while Seattle will look to rebound after getting spanked by the Bears at home. This should be a get-right game for the Seahawks, and a potential let-down affair for the Cardinals (who had a let-down two weeks ago, after their big upset win over the Rams in Week 6). Seattle may struggle to run the ball against a defense that just stymied Javonte Williams. Arizona has allowed the most TD passes (18) and second most FPPG to opposing QBs. Lock is ranked as the QB29 this week, which is absurd. He's behind Fagnano, Gardner Minshew II, and Shedeur Sanders? No. None of them have JSN to throw to. Obviously, he shouldn't be started over a Top-12 option, but I like him as a QB2 this week. In his only start of the season in Week 2, he put up 21 fantasy points at…you guessed it…Arizona.

Elite options this week – Josh Allen, Lamar, Drake Maye, Brock Purdy, Jared Goff, and Joe Burrow;the analysis starts below them.

Three QBs ranked in the Top-10 this week who should smash:

Dak Prescott (@IND). The Colts have given up, or at least, they don't have the ability to compete at the moment. Dak should have his way with this mediocre defense, which has allowed the tenth most FPPG to opposing QBs. Dallas can't afford another stupid loss.

Trevor Lawrence (@BAL). The Ravens have a decent defense that's middle of the pack against opposing QBs, but there's almost no scenario where they don't lose this game by a lot. Their offense won't be able to stay on the field, so Lawrence should have plenty of scoring chances.

Patrick Mahomes (vs. NYJ). Mahomes threw for 321 yards and three TDs at Denver, to break a mini-cold streak and this week he gets a much softer defense, at home. Kenneth Walker might run wild, but I think Mahomes will get his too. The Jets have allowed 14 TD passes this season and have only one pick (ahem, that's since the start of last season).

Malik Willis (vs. DET) is in play this week as a low-end QB1 streamer. He's thrown for two TDs in two straight games and added a rushing TD in Week 7. The Lions' defense is showing improvement over the last two weeks, but they're still vulnerable and while they got their safeties back, now they're without stalwart LB Jack Campbell. They've allowed the most passing yards, passing TDs (18) and FPPG to opposing QBs, and they've also yielded two rushing TDs to the position.

Other QB2-types to consider if you need it: C.J. Stroud (@LAC), Kirk Cousins (@SF), Bo Nix (@CAR), and J.J. McCarthy (@PHI). Carson Wentz (@BUF) is risky, but it's an excellent matchup.

There are some higher-end starters with less-than-ideal matchups, but none are bad enough for me to list them as Fades. However, there are some QB2s who fantasy managers could be considering this week, but that I'm avoiding if I can: Baker Mayfield (@CHI, and I'm not sure how much he can really throw right now), and Michael Penix, Jr. (vs. CIN, and if you started him last week, I'm sorry for your loss).

And don't start Justin Herbert (vs. HOU). This is now a running joke.

Elite options this week – Jahmyr Gibbs, Bijan Robinson, Kenneth Walker, and Christian McCaffrey; the analysis starts below them.

Three high-end backs in smash spots this week:

Javonte Williams (@IND) barely averaged two yards per carry last week, a week after setting a career high in rushing yards. Which Williams will we get this week? A good one, I expect. Indianapolis's run defense isn't as stout without DeForest Buckner and allows the tenth most FPPG to opposing RBs. The Colts are reeling and offered very little resistance to Jacksonville's ground game last week (the first without Buckner). Williams is among the league leaders in carries inside the five and should bounce back nicely in this spot.

Bucky Irving (@CHI) and Bhayshul Tuten (@BAL) are coming off monster games and should rack up some good totals again this week. Chicago is tougher up the gut with Jeffery Simmons in the middle, but Tampa will run it and then run it some more. The Ravens are a tough run defense, but game script is a big factor here. Jacksonville is a 10-point favorite and should dominate time of possession and play with a big lead. If Tuten is out, Chris Rodriguez, Jr. is an OK play if you need him.

Others ranked inside the Top-25 this week who I think will outperform their rankings: Chase Brown (@ATL, who now allow the third most FPPG to opposing RBs after getting run over for three straight hours on Sunday), Chuba Hubbard (vs. DEN), Jonah Coleman (@CAR, allowing the second most FPPG to opposing RBs), and Quinshon Judkins (@NO, most FPPG allowed to opposing RBs). If Chase Brown can't get the ground game going this week, I'll start to get worried.

A few to consider if you're stuck: Woody Marks (@LAC), Alvin Kamara (vs. CLE), Mike Washington, Jr. (@SF), Dylan Sampson (vs. CLE), and J.K. Dobbins (@CAR).

I've got some household names on the list this week.

Derrick Henry (vs. JAC) is a tough call this week. The Ravens are in a terrible situation for this game, on a short week. Jacksonville has been a buzzsaw for running backs. They allow the fewest yards and FPPG to opposing RBs. Henry is outside my Top-15 for the week. Henry scored last week, but his fantasy output has taken a huge hit since Lamar Jackson got hurt.

Is Jonathan Taylor (vs. DAL) really going to be active on Sunday? It's a tasty matchup (see, e.g., Allgeier, Tyler), but if he plays he could see limited snaps while potential buyers consider their offers and the Colts try to protect the asset. Start him if he's active, but with caution.

It's tough to start Saquon Barkley (@NYG), who is dealing with multiple injuries and isn't a lock to get a decent workload. Yes, he has two straight games with a TD, but in each of those games he played fewer than 20 snaps. I'm sure he still gets a little extra motivation against his old team, but they've been pretty good against running backs (Top-12 fantasy run defense).

Josh Jacobs (@NE) struggled to find running room last week and looked slow. He got bailed out with two short touchdowns, and that's his upside. Green Bay hasn't run the ball well all season, and New England allows the fifth fewest FPPG to opposing RBs. Pass.

The Seahawks struggled to run the ball consistently against Chicago, and it won't be all that much easier against Arizona, who is decent vs. the run. Throw in that Seattle is using multiple backs in rotation and I'll fade Zach Charbonnet and Jadarian Price (vs. ARI).

PSA: Don't start any Chargers RBs. Of all players who don't have injury as an excuse, Omarion Hampton is currently the single most disappointing player who went in the first two rounds of fantasy drafts.

Other RB Fades: Cam Skattebo (@PHI, if he plays), Ashton Jeanty (@SF), and Blake Corum (@WAS).

Elite options this week – Puka Nacua, Jaxon Smith-Njigba, Ja'Marr Chase AND Tee Higgins, Amon-Ra St. Brown, CeeDee Lamb, and Malik Nabers; the analysis starts below them.

I've listed seven "elites" above. Beyond them, here are six Rides this week, all ranked inside the weekly Top-20: Christian Watson AND Matthew Golden (@NE, tough matchup but they can't run the ball and will have to throw a lot), Parker Washington (to bounce back, @BAL), Rashee Rice (vs. NYJ), Justin Jefferson (vs. BUF, they've allowed the most TD catches and fifth most FPPG to opposing WRs, and the Vikings will actually need to score to win this game), and Luther Burden III (vs. TB).

Moving down to those ranked between 21 and 30 for the Week — I like these guys as WR2/WR3 or Flex plays: D.J. Moore (@MIN, tough matchup, but Moore will get a lot of 1-on-1s), Stefon Diggs (vs. LAR), Ladd McConkey (vs. HOU), and Mike Evans (vs. LV, assuming he plays).

WRs ranked outside the Top-30 this week that I think you can plug into lineups if you're in need comprise a pretty big list this week: Deebo Samuel, Sr. (vs. LV, if he plays), K.C. Concepcion, Jr. (@NO), Malik Washington AND Caleb Douglas (vs. DET), Jordan Addison (vs. BUF), Rome Odunze (vs. TB, he's making a few big catches each week and is overdue for a score), Makai Lemon (vs. NYG, and Joe Milton could be a nice upgrade for this passing game), and Ryan Flournoy (@IND).

Tet McMillan (vs. DEN) had a monster game in Week 4 but hasn't topped 12.5 fantasy points in the rest of his outings. The Broncos are a tough matchup, too (sixth fewest FPPG and just four TD catches allowed to the position).

The Jets have more weapons these days, so Garrett Wilson (@KC) is not seeing his usual massive target share. It's a tough matchup (second fewest FPPG and a league-low two TD catches allowed to opposing receivers).

I know he scored a TD and got a 2-point catch last week, but Jaylen Waddle (@CAR) is hard to trust. He played a season-low 45% of snaps last week, a troubling development. He also only had 39 receiving yards and was bailed out by the scoring plays.

I'll fade A.J. Brown (vs. GB) if he plays. He's an auto-fade to me until he looks like some semblance of himself.

#2881. Walker Kessler admitted Domantas Sabonis outplayed him before strong fourth quarter

Prior to Wednesday's fourth quarter, Walker Kessler was sitting on five points, three rebounds and two steals — far lesser production than he'd proven capable of thus far in this season. Then, he exploded for a 10-point, five-rebound, four-block, one-steal final frame to finish with 15 points, eight rebounds, four blocks and one steal. Those are numbers much more in line with the 25-year-old's early season signature.

Kessler's counterpart in the middle was Sacramento Kings center Domantas Sabonis. He's a rugged, brawny big man who's unafraid to launch elbows and hips into defenders for space down low and also threads passes to teammates for easy scores. Before that fourth-quarter renaissance, Kessler recognized the Lithuanian veteran — who logged 16 points, 17 rebounds (six on the offensive glass), one assist and one steal — was getting the best of him.

"He's a very, very talented player. Really physical player," Kessler told reporters after the Los Angeles Lakers' 116-103 win on Wednesday. "Definitely got my tail kicked a little bit in the first three quarters. But I relied on my teammates and they did a good job of encouraging me … At the end of the day, just trying to win and started to roll my way a little bit."

While likely past his All-NBA apex at this stage of his career, the 30-year-old Sabonis remains a good player and tough cover. For Kessler to struggle through three quarters but still stay in the fight mentally to bounce back and help fuel a third straight Lakers victory is encouraging. Of course, the white-hot exploits of Luka Dončić, who notched his fourth consecutive 40-point outing, didn't ding Los Angeles' win probability either.

Kessler will face another big, strong body Friday night when Donovan Clingan and the West-leading, 6-1 Portland Trail Blazers come to town. It'll mark both the Lakers' second meeting of the year with Portland and their second NBA Cup Group Play game. After blowing a big lead to the Golden State Warriors last Friday in their opening NBA Cup matchup of 2026-27, they must win this one to maintain any hope of advancing to the Quarterfinals. Tipoff is schedule for 7 p.m.

#2880. The 1 trade Bengals must make in final week before 2026 NFL trade deadline

The 1 trade Bengals must make in final week before 2026 NFL trade deadline appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Do the Cincinnati Bengals have one more trade in them this season? They've already made one of the biggest trades this season by acquiring ex-New York Giants defensive tackle Dexter Lawrence during the 2026 NFL Draft. Despite that blockbuster move, though, Cincinnati isn't quite satisfied with where their team currently stands amongst the contenders in the AFC.

Because of that, the Bengals are involved in some trade rumors as the trade deadline approaches. There are plenty of trades that Cincinnati can make to bolster their roster. Let's look at the move that this team should make.

The Bengals traded for Lawrence, yes, but it was clear that the defense still had a ton of weaknesses. In particular, their linebacker group was a major question mark coming into the season. Barrett Carter and Demetrius Knight Jr. have both played better this season, but it's still painfully clear that both linebackers are outmatched against their opponents. Opposing offenses have feasted in the short and intermediate areas of the field due to the duo's struggles in coverage.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

That's the reason why Bengals fans were clamoring for the team to sign or trade for a linebacker during the offseason. Popular choices at the time include Dolphins LB Jordyn Brooks and now-Washington Commanders LB Leo Chenal. However, another more intriguing option has emerged this season as a potential trade target: Philadelphia Eagles linebacker Zack Baun.

One of the best linebackers in the league, Baun's availability is tied heavily to the Eagles' fate this season. With Jalen Hurts out for the year due to his deep vein thrombosis, Philadelphia has gone from contenders to potential sellers at the trade deadline. Tanner McKee has done nothing to inspire confidence in a playoff run, and they're now taking a look at prospect Joe Milton to lead them the rest of the day. Because of that and the long-term implications of Hurts' DVT diagnosis, many are wondering if Philadelphia might decide to enter a rebuild.

Baun is one of the players who will likely fetch good value at the trade deadline. The Eagles linebacker started his career as an edge rusher, but he has made the transition to a coverage linebacker with the Eagles. Baun is one of the best coverage linebackers in the league, able to cover both tight ends and slot receivers in space. He was part of the 2024 Eagles team that won the Super Bowl, so he also brings championship pedigree to a Bengals team that has high aspirations this season.

Cincinnati's linebackers are playing better than last year, but last year is an admittedly low standard that they have barely cleared this season. Knight Jr. has been great against the run this season, but he's one of the worst linebackers in coverage this year. Carter has been great for the most part too, but he's still prone to lapses in coverage and takes bad angles at times. Having Baun, a bona fide veteran, as one of their linebackers would be greatly helpful to this team moving forward.

#2877. Lakers’ Austin Reaves turns over new leaf in road victory

SACRAMENTO — Austin Reaves accomplished a feat for just the second time this season during the Lakers' 116-103 win over the Kings on Wednesday.

And he wasn't afraid to let a reporter know about it after his team's third straight win to improve to 7-2.

Lakers guard Austin Reaves knows the areas he's struggling with to start this season. AP Photo/Scott Marshall

The Lakers star guard recorded 21 points, four assists and three rebounds in the victory over his team's in-state foe.

But the biggest stat that stood out: zero turnovers — something Reaves had done in just one other game so far.

"You got something to say? You can take credit for it," Reaves quipped postgame to a reporter who brought up Reaves' struggles with turnovers pregame. "Go ahead. Talking crazy before the game."

A friendly, lighthearted back-and-forth broke out between Reaves and the reporter, who asked again how many turnovers the 6-foot-5 guard had.

Wednesday's win over the Kings was the second time Lakers star guard Austin Reaves didn't record a turnover. NBAE via Getty Images

It was five, to be exact, with Reaves struggling to take care of the ball during the Lakers' win over the Heat on Monday.

The only other game Reaves played this season without a turnover was the Oct. 27 loss to the Trail Blazers.

Was Wednesday the first step in Austin Reaves turning around his ball security issues? NBAE via Getty Images

Ball security has been an issue for him throughout the season.

His 4.1 turnovers per game are not only a career high but also tied for the NBA's third-highest figure.

He averaged six turnovers in the Lakers' previous four games before Wednesday.

"I've had a lot all year," he said. "We're gonna fix those. For you."

Was Wednesday the start of Reaves fixing his ball security woes?

Friday's home game against the Trail Blazers will provide insight into whether that'll be true.

#2875. Yaxel Lendeborg has changed everything for Steph Curry, Warriors this season

This was supposed to be a wasted season for Steph Curry.

The Warriors were injury-riddled and coming off a lackluster summer in which they made no major moves.

It meant the greatest shooter of all time was destined to tread water as he turned 39 years old.

He has resuscitated Curry's career. He has breathed new life into a flailing dynasty. He has given the Warriors their future franchise star.

Lendeborg was a question mark when the Warriors selected him as the No. 11 pick in the 2026 draft.

Can a jack-of-all-trades turn into a star? Can a 24-year-old surpass expectations or has he already peaked? Can a rookie really be NBA ready?

Lendeborg has emphatically answered those questions.

He's averaging 21.5 points (50% shooting from the field, 40% from 3), seven rebounds and 3.9 assists for the Warriors, who have won five games in a row.

Golden State Warriors' Yaxel Lendeborg dribbles against the Phoenix Suns during an NBA game at Chase Center in San Francisco, Monday, Nov. 2, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

The Warriors are good. Curry has a wingman. This is thrilling for anyone who loves basketball.

No one seems to be savoring this more than Curry, whose joy has been on full display amid the Warriors' unexpected rise.

During his 44-point deluge against the Timberwolves on Oct. 28, Curry flashed a lunge pose after sinking a shot from beyond the arc.

After his jab sent Luka Doncic onto his butt and he swished a 3-pointer against the Lakers on Oct. 30, he did the Edvard Munch celebration, putting his hands on his cheeks and opening his mouth in shock.

And after scoring 34 points against the Suns on Monday, he didn't hesitate when asked what has changed for the Warriors this season.

"I mean, Yax," Curry said, flashing a smile.

Golden State Warriors guard Steph Curry warms up before a game against the Los Angeles Lakers in Los Angeles, Wednesday, Oct. 21, 2026. London Entertainment

Curry and Lendeborg are the NBA's second-highest scoring duo (51.5 points per game), behind Luka Dončić and Austin Reaves (61.8). Lendeborg's success has been a life raft for the Warriors, who are without Jimmy Butler and Moses Moody for the first few months of the season and without Kristaps Porzingis indefinitely.

In many ways, Lendeborg has unlocked Curry. He has made his brilliance count. He has lit a new fire under him.

Curry, who missed 27 straight games because of runner's knee last season, is averaging 30 points (46.8% shooting from the field, 42.9% from beyond the arc), 4.2 rebounds and 4.5 assists per game.

He just surpassed Michael Jordan (44) for the most 40-point games in NBA history since turning 30, notching his 45th such performance last week.

Curry is playing as though he were a decade younger. And that's because a guy 14 years younger than him is making all of this meaningful.

It's funny because this really couldn't have worked out better for both parties.

Curry, a two-time MVP and four-time champion, is doing what he loves most: winning.

And the outspoken Lendeborg has found a team that needs him to contribute immediately and encourages his bravado, two things that could've been problematic with other franchises.

Yaxel Lendeborg #1 of the Golden State Warriors drives to the basket against Jalen Green #4 of the Phoenix Suns in San Francisco on Nov. 2, 2026. Getty Images

And neither is being politically correct.

The rookie recently raised eyebrows when he stunningly said he lost all respect for Dončić following their trash talk in the season opener. The Warriors didn't wince.

Instead, Curry, Draymond Green and coach Steve Kerr unapologetically had his back.

Lendeborg landed in the perfect situation. He has a mentor in Green, who's famous for unabashedly speaking his mind. And he has a franchise behind him with a win-now mentality that needs him to shine.

Lendeborg has found his home. He can flourish here. He needs to flourish here.

It's in the form of a rookie who has made it so that instead of treading water he's soaring.

#2874. Sacred Heart Academy claims SCC volleyball title over Amity behind JJ Kirck's 31 digs

WEST HAVEN - It is often said that offense wins games and defense wins championships. Sacred Heart Academy senior libero JJ Kirck will attest to that.

Playing spectacular defense, including 31 digs, Kirck was one of the keys in leading the second-seeded Sharks to a 3-2 (21-25, 25-11, 25-19, 23-25, 15-11) victory over top-seeded Amity in the Southern Connecticut Conference championship game at West Haven High on Thursday evening.

"Our team battled all year," Kirck said after being named the tournament's Most Outstanding Player. "We played so many different games this year, and every game besides one, we came out on top. Tonight was just a show of how much we faced adversity."

The Sharks avenged consecutive SCC championship game losses to Amity and improved to 20-1 on the season.

"It means everything to me," Kirck said. "I'm from Amity. I love all these girls across the net. I grew up playing volleyball with all of them. I am so glad my senior year, I was able to get the dub, and I am so glad I was able to do it with my best friends."

Both teams entered the contest without losing a set thus far in the SCC tournament. Amity, which has been in the final every year since 2021 and won two in a row over the Sharks, delivered the first blow in handing Sacred Heart Academy its first set loss of the tournament by riding the play of Calleigh Parkin and Brooke Cichon in the opening set.

The Sharks pushed back and dominated the second set with a 14-point win that saw a kill by Aliya Church put the Sharks ahead 16-8 and her ace extend the lead to 20-9.

"Their defense played really well," Amity coach Seth Davis said after his team fell to 19-3 on the year. "They dig a lot of balls. They pass really well. Once we started passing, we could keep the ball in system and get the ball to Olive (Hubbard)."

The two teams used strong defensive play to split the next two sets as Kirck's defense thwarted Amity in the third set before she closed it out with a pair of service points.

The Spartans would not go quietly, overcoming a 21-19 fourth-set deficit with a pair of kills by SCC Player of the Year Olive Hubbard, a kill by Parkin, and a block by Hubbard to close the set.

"We did our job from the service line," Sacred Heart Academy coach Dave Alexandro said. "Tough serves to the seams, and it kind of took them out of system which allowed us to get some swings on the ball, and then build up a little bit of a lead. With that confidence, swinging well, hitting the ball well, kind of moving them around the court made a big difference."

The turning point came in the fifth set when a block by Paige Nelson gave Sacred Heart Academy a 10-9 lead. The Sharks never looked back as a kill by Church increased the lead to 12-9 before Isabella Pellegrino sealed the championship with a kill.

Bella Spinato was dominant for Sacred Heart Academy with 43 assists. Pellegrino finished with 25 kills, and Church added 16 kills.

Cichon had 16 kills and 17 digs, and Hubbard finished with 15 kills in the loss for Amity.

JJ Kirck, Sacred Heart Academy: The senior libero had 31 digs as her team topped Amity 3-2 to win the SCC title.

"I know wherever I get the ball up, my setter is going to set it perfectly. My job is really easy. All I have to do is get the ball off the floor." - Sacred Heart Academy's JJ Kirck.

#2871. What is Rams’ plan for Ty Simpson?

The Los Angeles Rams had a plan to futureproof the quarterback position. They hid their intentions well during the spring before shocking the NFL by selecting Ty Simpson with the thirteenth overall pick.

But a plan—at least a good one—doesn't stop at drafting a player.

We knew Simpson was a green prospect entering the draft. He looked as ready as you possibly could during the preseason. For someone who the consensus said needed time to develop, Simpson looked poised under pressure and kept the ball out of harm's way during the exhibition games.

Through eight weeks Simpson has been a healthy scratch on game days. He still dressed as the emergency third quarterback behind Matthew Stafford and Stetson Bennett, although he cannot step onto the field unless the two signal callers ahead of him are unavailable because of injury.

It's time to reassess whether Simpson should be the third quarterback. That is not to say that Simpson should be starting over Stafford, although just two weeks ago it was difficult to see LA climbing out of the hole they put themselves in.

I have a hard time believing that Bennett is more capable and dependable than Simpson. It's probably time for the rookie to usurp the veteran within the depth chart. Stafford is also managing an elbow injury that requires him to frequently ice his elbow, which could help explain his decline in play this season.

It's worth mentioning: the Rams owe Bennett nothing. His contract is set to expire at season's end. It would take more money to retain him, which the team probably won't do. The most likely outcome is that Bennett goes to team that runs a similar offensive scheme to Sean McVay and where he has a chance to get onto the field.

So if the Rams are headed towards a separation with Bennett, why not give Simpson any reps as the number two quarterback and allow him to backup Stafford on gamedays?

It could be that the team is waiting for their bye week to make a change. That comes after the next two games.

Still, we know the Rams have a plan to develop Simpson. The details are simply unclear.

At some point the rookie will replace Bennett as the backup. It's really just a matter of when. Why not right now?

#2869. Why Troy Aikman Doesn’t Deserve Sympathy Over His ESPN Contract Situation

Somebody check on Troy Aikman. Maybe send flowers. Start a GoFundMe. Have Joe Buck organize a benefit concert. Apparently, we're supposed to feel sorry for a guy making $18 million a year because his employer hasn't offered him another contract to talk about football. Somewhere in America, somebody is working a double shift, wondering how they're going to pay the mortgage, while we're discussing whether Aikman feels betrayed because Joe Buck got a new deal.

Forgive me if I don't reach for a tissue.

Aikman is finishing a reported five-year, $90 million contract with ESPN. That's $18 million annually. Divide that by 17 Monday Night Football games, and it's roughly $1.06 million per Monday night. Yes, he prepares, travels and works postseason games. That's not his literal game check. But however you divide it, that's a pretty nice living for explaining why somebody missed a tackle.

Meanwhile, Buck reportedly received a six-year, $108 million extension, and Aikman didn't. Now we're hearing about hurt feelings, reported disagreements and whether their 25-season television partnership is headed for divorce. Apparently, Buck is supposed to save his partner. ESPN is supposed to reward loyalty, and the rest of us are supposed to feel terrible that Aikman might have to explore free agency.

The horror. Maybe he'll have to update his LinkedIn profile.

Look, Aikman has had a remarkable life. Three Super Bowl championships, a Hall of Fame career and approximately $55.5 million in NFL playing earnings. Then came 21 seasons at FOX Sports, where he reportedly made around $7.5 million annually toward the end of his run. Then negotiated an extension worth $13.2 million a year before leaving for ESPN. Add his current five-year, $90 million ESPN contract, and the Cowboys and ESPN alone account for more than $145 million.

We don't know exactly what FOX Sports paid him over those two decades, but factor in those years. In addition to endorsements, appearances and business ventures. It's entirely plausible his career gross earnings have approached or exceeded $250 million. That's a quarter of a billion dollars for playing football, talking about football and selling things to people who enjoy football.

I've had Aikman on my radio shows through endorsement relationships. He's made an extraordinary living, and good for him. But let's not confuse a wealthy broadcaster approaching the end of a contract with somebody standing in the unemployment line.

And why exactly is this Joe Buck's problem?

Buck and Aikman have worked together for 25 seasons. That's impressive. So is being married for 25 years. But plenty of couples eventually decide they'd rather watch television in separate rooms. Buck negotiated a terrific deal. Why should he apologize or jeopardize his own future because ESPN hasn't offered Aikman another enormous contract?

Buck isn't his agent, accountant or emotional support broadcaster. He's the play-by-play guy.

Besides, can we stop acting as though we're about to lose Madden and Summerall? Buck and Aikman are fine. Comfortable. Familiar. Like a restaurant you've been visiting for 25 years because you know exactly what you're getting.

Now we're even discussing whether Aikman will call the Super Bowl. Andrew Marchand of The Athletic recently estimated a 95% chance he works ESPN's first Super Bowl and a 5% chance he doesn't. Five percent? Why is there any percentage devoted to this conversation?

He's under contract through the game. It's the Super Bowl, not an optional company picnic where the potato salad might be questionable.

To be fair, Aikman hasn't threatened to skip it. He's indicated he intends to finish his assignments. Good. That's what he's supposed to do.

However, the speculation has become ridiculous. Enough with the fascination over whether Buck and Aikman can remain professional. People are treating this like The Real Housewives of Monday Night Football. Hurt feelings, awkward looks and contract drama. All we're missing is somebody throwing a drink. Did Buck look irritated? Did Aikman roll his eyes? Is somebody getting voted off the island? Give me a break.

These are grown men making $18 million a year. Everybody has worked with somebody they weren't particularly happy with. Most of us didn't get a million dollars to sit next to them for three hours.

I side with ESPN. If the network wants Aikman back, that's its decision. If it wants somebody younger, more energetic or simply different, that's its decision, too. Monday Night Football should be a destination job, not a lifetime appointment.

Sunday Night Football has become the NFL's premier weekly showcase, but ESPN has an opportunity to make Monday nights special again.

Greg Olsen deserves consideration. Peyton Manning would be fascinating. Pat McAfee would be a gamble, but at least an interesting one. Just don't repeat the Jason Witten experiment by handing the job to somebody because he has a recognizable name. Find somebody who has earned it, can explain the game and makes the broadcast better.

Aikman can negotiate whatever he believes he's worth. ESPN can decide whether to pay it. Buck can enjoy his new contract without being asked to rescue his partner. Nobody is being wronged here. It's business. Sometimes business means somebody decides you're not worth what you think you are.

Troy Aikman spent his playing career winning Super Bowls and his broadcasting career earning enormous checks. He's had one of the great runs in sports. But being successful doesn't make you indispensable, and being disappointed doesn't make you a victim.

So please, save the sympathy. Save the candlelight vigil. Save the GoFundMe.

Don't worry. After nearly four decades of playing football and talking about it, I don't think Aikman will have to start driving for Uber. Unless, of course, Joe Buck needs a ride.

#2867. Drake Maye vs. Jordan Love: Which is the better quarterback?

We here at Acme Packing Company are careful not to judge quarterbacks on raw counting statistics unless they suit our needs. For instance, we love Brett Favre's longevity stats, yards, and touchdowns, but when it comes to interceptions, for all QBs with at least 2500 attempts, he's 75th (out of 147). Not too shabby! But usually, especially lately, counting stats have worked against us because the Packers run at SUCH a slow pace that Jordan Love really can't rack up any significant yardage or touchdown totals. And so, typically we rely on things like EPA (Expected Points Added) and CPOE (Completion Percentage Over Expected), which makes us feel all warm and fuzzy.

But fast forward to THIS season and suddenly Jordan Love LEADS THE LEAGUE IN PASSING YARDS. And also attempts, which is not as good, but still noteworthy. This is also new and fascinating, because we've all been asking to let Jordan cook for quite a while, but with all that volume, plus a trash running game (and some other factors, which we will get to), he hasn't really been himself from an efficiency standpoint. He's still more efficient than an average quarterback with .15 EPA per play (down significantly from .289 last year), and so the offense still works, but it's a big drop. On that point, the only quarterback who had a better EPA per play last season was this week's opponent, Drake Maye (.322).

Maye is in his third season, and unquestionably great, but just how great is still a bit of a mystery. Last year he was probably the best quarterback in football, leading the way in EPA per play and the NextGenStats' version of CPOE, which is widely regarded as the most accurate version. But like Love, Maye didn't pass that much, finishing 12th in attempts with 492 (Love was 19th with 439, but he also missed two games. They were very close on a per-game basis).

Maye's numbers are still good this year as he's third among active starters in EPA per play (.279, down almost .05 from last year), but his volume has ALSO fallen as he ranks 17th in pass attempts behind players like Geno Smith and CJ Stroud. His CPOE has also decreased by over 50%, and he ranks seventh instead of first. Maye is, I think, having an objectively better season than Love in all the ways that matter from an individual player perspective. However, Love has almost certainly been the more valuable player, because when you are adding value to a football team, volume absolutely matters.

One of the big reasons the Patriots have thrown fewer passes than the Packers is because (how best to put this), the Patriots' running game doesn't suck as much as Green Bay's. None of this is hyperbole, and it's important to understand that both running games do in fact suck. The Pats as a unit have run 240 times for a 4.1 YPC average, however, that average is dragged up by Drake Maye scrambles. The top two Patriots running backs, TreVeyon Henderson and Rhamondre Stephenson, both average under four yards per carry, and have combined for 148 carries. The Packers as a unit average a ridiculously terrible 2.9 yards per carry, and as a result, they've only run the ball 170 times. The Patriots are definitely better, and they have definitely been better running AND passing without question. Their offense, by any account, should be MUCH more effective; however, in the aggregate, the Patriots only average 1.6 more points per game than Green Bay (please keep Trey Smack in mind while reading this) simply because the Packers pass more.

When it's all said and done, it seems like Maye is still the clear winner here, and I won't argue with anyone who makes this claim. He's an outstanding young player. But even so, it's not quite so cut and dried. Both quarterbacks have suffered this season compared to last season based on factors outside of their control. Love has suffered from a terrible offensive line and running game. Maye has lost Romeo Doubs and AJ Brown for extended periods, and his protection has struggled as well. And for Love, there is the added issue of drops.

The Packers have an insane number of drops. Matthew Golden leads the league in drops with 9, and overall, the Packers lead the league with a drop rate of a robust 7% of targets. Those are balls that Jordan Love has put on target (often for what should be big gains) and just don't happen. And on the other side, Patriots pass catchers have been some of the most reliable in the league, dropping just 2.5% of targets.

Love's efficiency numbers would be substantially higher with an average number of drops instead of whatever this is. Would it be enough to catch Maye? Ehhh, maybe not, but given how often the Packers are throwing the ball, it would certainly be enough to push the Packers' offense, in the aggregate, past the Pats.

In the end, it doesn't really matter if Maye is better or if Love is better. I think the real lesson is that quarterbacks really are impacted significantly by their surrounding cast, their volume, and their coaching, and that we can never really truly remove all of that context. Secondarily, it's important to account for that context when making predictions! I'm not sure that Matthew Golden is going to start catching everything, and I'm worried that Josh Jacobs' return is going to lead to more pointless running, but there is a path to victory here for Green Bay. Hopefully they take it.

#2862. Preview: Klay returns to Chase Center with new look Heat

It's a great time for sports fans to be in the Bay Area, Dub Nation! The Valkyries won their first WNBA title on Halloween night at Chase Center and paraded the trophy down the Embarcadero yesterday. And tonight, the building that just hosted a championship celebration hosts the Miami Heat and a quasi homecoming for a guy who earned a few of those parades himself. Wassup, Klay Thompson!

The Warriors remember the last time Miami came to Chase. Jimmy Butler went down with a torn ACL that night, and he's still out tonight, along with Kristaps Porzingis, Moses Moody and Gui Santos.

Yesterday while San Francisco was sweeping violet confetti off the Embarcadero, Klay Thompson was in Inglewood remembering exactly who he is. He dropped 30 on 8-of-17 from deep to cap a 20-point comeback over the Clippers. Down two in overtime, Giannis grabbed a defensive rebound with 3.8 seconds left, Miami called timeout, and on the ensuing inbound Giannis found Klay open in the left corner off a Bam screen for the walk-off three. Per a stat making the rounds, it was the first game-winning three of Klay's career in the final five seconds of the fourth quarter or OT, regular season and playoffs. Klay's got four rings, 37 points in a quarter, Game 6 in OKC, and yet the walk-off three was the one trinket missing from the shelf.

The Warriors he's coming back to look nothing like the team that started 0-3. They've since beaten the Nuggets, Timberwolves, Lakers, Clippers and Suns, including the Clippers win with both Steph and Draymond sitting out, and Monday's win over Phoenix came after trailing by 17.

The South Beach super team rolls out Davion Mitchell, Thompson, Andrew Wiggins, Giannis Antetokounmpo and Bam Adebayo. Wiggins also won a title with the Warriors back in 2022 and got into the parade action; he joins Klay on the walk to the familiar arena.

Miami's journey so far hasn't been smooth. Miami lost its first four before winning three of the next four, and in that Clippers game everybody not named Klay went 2-of-23 from deep.

Through eight games, Miami ranks dead last in the league in opponent threes made (17.1 per game) and opponent three-point percentage (40.5 percent), which is an engraved invitation for a Steph Curry who's averaging 30 a night. The flip side is a Golden State frontcourt that starts Draymond at center with rookie Yaxel Lendeborg at forward, staring down a two-time MVP and a Bam who just set season highs with 35 points and 10 rebounds. Miami's been getting nearly 20 points a night at the free throw line, so Lendeborg has to wall up Giannis without fouling. When the Warriors shoot, he has to find Giannis and put a body on him before the Greek Freak can turn a long rebound into a one-man fast break. Then there's the Steve Kerr and Erik Spoelstra subplot, two guys who drew up gold-medal sets on the same whiteboard in Paris now trying to burn each other's playbooks.

The schedule does hand Golden State an edge. The Warriors had two days off, while the Heat are on the second night of a back-to-back and their third game in four nights, and Klay's status is still uncertain. If he plays, Chase will rain love on him during introductions, and then 18,000 people will spend the next two hours praying he left his new walk-off habit in Inglewood.

#2861. Browns Cut Rookie One Day After Promoting Him

PROVO, UT - AUGUST 30: Carsen Ryan #20 of the Brigham Young Cougars scores a touchdown agaisnt the Portland State Vikings during the first half of their game at LaVell Edwards Stadium on August 30, 2025 in Provo, Utah. (Photo by Chris Gardner/Getty Images) Getty

NASHVILLE, TENNESSEE - OCTOBER 25: Harold Fannin Jr. #44 of the Cleveland Browns runs the ball against Marcus Harris #26 of the Tennessee Titans during the fourth quarter at Nissan Stadium on October 25, 2026 in Nashville, Tennessee. (Photo by Andy Lyons/Getty Images) Getty

The Cleveland Browns reversed course on rookie tight end Carsen Ryan one day after bringing him back to the active roster.

Cleveland announced Thursday that it had waived Ryan, a seventh-round pick who was promoted from the practice squad Wednesday. The move came as the Browns prepared for their Week 9 matchup with the New Orleans Saints.

The team's announcement provided no explanation for the decision and did not include a corresponding addition. Ryan's sudden exit drew the attention of Browns reporter Zac Jackson, who questioned what might follow.

"This indicates they need a spot for someone else, so curious what's next," Jackson wrote on X.

The Browns had signed Ryan to the active roster Wednesday while adding running back Hassan Haskins to the practice squad. One day later, Ryan was back on waivers. His release creates another roster decision for general manager Andrew Berry during a week already dominated by uncertainty at quarterback.

GettyCarsen Ryan #20 of BYU scores a touchdown agaisnt the Portland State Vikings during the first half of their game at LaVell Edwards Stadium on August 30, 2025 in Provo, Utah.

Thursday's transaction continued a busy stretch for the BYU product. Cleveland previously waived Ryan on October 26 when it activated quarterback Dillon Gabriel from injured reserve. Ryan subsequently returned to the practice squad and was elevated for the Week 8 game in Pittsburgh before Wednesday's promotion.

He has appeared in seven games this season, although his role has primarily come on special teams.

The Browns selected Ryan with the No. 248 overall pick in April. He finished his college career at BYU after previous stops at UCLA and Utah.

During his final college season, Ryan caught 45 passes for 620 yards and three touchdowns in 14 games. That production helped him earn a chance to develop in Cleveland's tight end room, though it has not yet translated into a regular role on offense. His earlier return to the practice squad shows that being waived did not end his time with the Browns. Whether Cleveland takes that route again remains to be seen

GettyHarold Fannin Jr. #44 of the Cleveland Browns runs the ball against Marcus Harris #26 of the Tennessee Titans during the fourth quarter at Nissan Stadium on October 25, 2026 in Nashville, Tennessee.

The timing of Ryan's release became more notable when Harold Fannin Jr. appeared on Thursday's injury report with a groin issue. Fannin was limited in practice. The Browns had not ruled him out against New Orleans or announced how much the injury could affect his workload.

Fannin and Blake Whiteheart are the remaining tight ends on Cleveland's active roster. John FitzPatrick is on the practice squad, and rookie Joe Royer remains on the reserve/non-football illness list.

Fannin is coming off a career-high 154 receiving yards against Pittsburgh, making his availability a much bigger immediate concern for the offense than Ryan's limited offensive role.

Cleveland's next injury report and any follow-up roster move should provide a clearer picture of the tight ends available for Sunday.

#2860. Drew Lock: My elbow feels good, felt great to rip some balls yesterday

Drew Lock has been dealing with an elbow injury, but he says it’s not impacting him as he readies to start against the Cardinals on Sunday.

While the Seahawks listed Lock as a limited participant on Wednesday’s injury report, he said his elbow is doing “a lot better.”

#2859. NFL Thursday Night Football preview: Ravens to start rookie Joe Fagnano at QB vs. Jaguars

The Baltimore Ravens had hope before kickoff last Sunday. Ahead of their kickoff on Thursday, most of that hope has vanished with two months left in the season.

The Ravens suffered not one but two quarterback injuries on Sunday. Lamar Jackson dislocated his right thumb on his first play back from an ankle injury, then Tyler Huntley injured his toe. Both players are on injured reserve.

Before last Sunday, you would have had to have watched a lot of preseason football to know Joe Fagnano was the Ravens' third-string quarterback. The undrafted rookie is now elevated to QB1 for their game on Thursday night against a very good Jacksonville Jaguars team. There is help on the way next week, as the Ravens reportedly were finalizing a deal for Cardinals quarterback Jacoby Brissett, but Fagnano will get at least one start.

Fagnano started his college career as a walk-on at Maine. He went to UConn after that and had an impressive 28-to-1 touchdown-to-interception ratio for the Huskies. He still went undrafted and signed with the Ravens with no money guaranteed. In his first NFL action last week, he completed 10-of-21 passes for 91 yards. He will benefit from getting starter's reps in practice, even though it's a short week. It's still a very tough assignment.

Baltimore Ravens quarterback Joe Fagnano made his NFL debut last week against Buffalo.

The Jaguars are 5-2 and lead the NFL with a +85 point differential. They're up to No. 2 in DVOA behind the 49ers and are the only team that ranks in the top-five of offense, defense and special teams in DVOA. Three of their five wins have come by 24 or more points. It looks like they'll run away with the AFC South.

The Ravens are just trying to stay alive in case Jackson can make a return sometime late in the season. They have to wait one more week for Brissett to be the bridge quarterback to that. This week, they're relying on an undrafted rookie to help pull off an improbable win.

It's reasonable to say the Ravens. If they win they'll be 5-4 with Jacoby Brissett coming on board. The season wouldn't technically be over at that point. However, Baltimore's chances of staying afloat over the second half of the season and also having Lamar Jackson return are slim. When you lose a two-time MVP quarterback to injured reserve, there's not much you can do about it. The Jaguars are cruising along at 5-2 and look like a legitimate Super Bowl contender. They might not be the more desperate team, but they're the one with a bright future this season. They need it more just because a loss to a reeling team with an undrafted rookie quarterback would poor cold water on the idea that the Jaguars can win a championship.

If the Ravens can pull off an upset, it will be because the defense plays great and the offense carries its quarterback. That formula puts a lot of pressure on Henry. It won't be easy against a Jaguars team that will likely come in not respecting the Ravens' passing game with a rookie making his first start. The Jaguars also allow the fewest rushing yards and rushing yards per carry in the NFL. That did come with defensive tackle DaVon Hamilton having a good season, and he suffered a season-ending quad injury last week. Henry had a good start to the season, but has had some quiet games lately with the Ravens suffering through bad quarterback play. He has 156 yards on 45 carries (3.5-yard average) over the last three weeks, and Baltimore hasn't scored more than 13 points in any of those games. But they need Henry to have a dominant performance Thursday, and that won't be easy against a good Jaguars defense.

The Jaguars travel to Baltimore to take on the M.A.S.H. unit that is the Ravens' remaining roster on Thursday night. Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley? Both out on IR. Third-stringer Joe Fagnano will start, but is dealing with an ankle injury. Baltimore is reportedly trading for Jacoby Brissett, but he won't be playing this week. To put a little context into the state of both teams, this line was Ravens -4.5 this offseason, it re-opened Ravens +8 on Sunday and got as high as +10. The Jaguars are only 4-3 against the spread this season, but they're coming off a 42-7 rout of an undermanned Colts team. Baltimore has talent on both sides of the ball, but this is a big ask for Fagnano — on a short week and with an injury no less. There are some Jacksonville -9.5s out there in the market. I'd jump on one of those or put Jacksonville in a 7-point tease with the 49ers -9.5 to get both under a field goal. — Ben Fawkes

Check out more of Ben's work at the Yahoo Sports betting hub.

Jacksonville at Baltimore looked like a signature game when the schedule was released, but all bets are off with Lamar Jackson unavailable. Here are three fantasy angles to track as you watch the game.

1. Can Zay Flowers survive? Flowers has shown the ability to produce with backups before — he was just fine with Tyler Huntley last year. But with Jackson and Huntley both unavailable and Jacoby Brissett a week away, the Ravens have to make do with undrafted rookie Joe Fagnano in this spot. Flowers did not have a catch in the loss to Buffalo.

2. What does the Jacksonville pass distribution look like? Although Trevor Lawrence attempted just 14 passes last week, he found a way to target eight different players. Parker Washington is the unquestioned No. 1 receiver in the Jacksonville huddle, but we'd like to see him draw a larger market share.

3. Will Derrick Henry find running lanes? Henry is finally showing some attrition near the end of his Hall of Fame career, sliding down to 4.0 YPC. The loss of Jackson obviously doesn't help, and he'll be challenged by a Jaguars defense that's No. 1 in run defense DVOA. The Ravens would like to give Henry 20 or more touches, and attempt to shorten the game. — Scott Pianowski

Since Kalimuendo has been limited to just 110 minutes across five appearances this season, Nottingham Forest's asking price may well have fallen since the summer.

Kalimuendo scored six goals in 19 appearances for Eintracht during his loan spell last season.

#2856. Game Preview: The spiraling Suns need a win tonight in the worst way

Who: Phoenix Suns (1-5) vs. Orlando Magic (2-5)

Where: Mortgage Matchup Center — Phoenix, Arizona

Watch: Arizona's Family 3TV, Arizona's Family Sports

The Suns are back at it tonight after a loss to the Trail Blazers last night. The homestand continues.

Four straight losses. 1-5. And the Suns just watched Portland drop 43 on them in a single quarter after winning the first one 34-29.

I'll say it out loud: game 7 of an 82-game season is a must-win. That sounds insane from the outside. It isn't. Houston and San Antonio come to town right after this, and if Phoenix can't beat a 2-5 Orlando team missing Paolo Banchero, I have no idea where the next win comes from.

Through six games, nothing on film says this team is better than its record. The ball movement in the first quarter in the last two games showed what the ceiling looks like. NBA games are 48 minutes long; it needs to be there for more than 12 or 15 minutes. If Jordan Ott's group can't defend a bottom-10 offense at home, the conversation shifts from "early-season growing pains" to "roster construction problem," and the lineup experimentation has to start now.

Mark Williams — OUT (Left Shoulder Surgery)

Jordan Goodwin — OUT (Right Calf Soreness)

Rasheer Fleming — QUESTIONABLE (Left Ankle Sprain)

The Suns need to play big, for once. Orlando has size.

Phoenix got out-rebounded by 8 against Portland and let the Blazers shoot 41 free throws. Donovan Clingan put up 24 and 11 inside. Portland was the more physical team, and the Suns settled instead of attacking. That's a size problem and a mentality problem at the same time.

OKLAHOMA CITY, OKLAHOMA – OCTOBER 26: Khaman Maluach #10 of the Phoenix Suns handles the ball during the first half against the Oklahoma City Thunder at Paycom Center on October 26, 2026 in Oklahoma City, Oklahoma. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Joshua Gateley/Getty Images) | Getty Images

Orlando brings real size back. Franz Wagner, Desmond Bane and Wendell Carter Jr. have a 105.9 defensive rating together over 155 minutes, which is elite. But the Magic are running a switch-heavy scheme with a small rookie guard in the starting five, and that's the mismatch Phoenix should live on.

A long, physical Suns wing on Richardson in the post is a free bucket every time Orlando switches. They need to hunt him relentlessly. The best stretch of the Portland game came when Phoenix went right at Lillard. Orlando hands them the same target. Anthony Black is another guy to watch, as he returns after missing a game.

Bench depth matters more than usual. It's the second night of a home-home back-to-back. Orlando is fresh from rest and starting a four-game road trip

Phoenix's defense has to show up. They are ranked 30th in the NBA in defensive rating at 124.5. The Magic have been held to under 100 points four times this season already. Orlando is 30th in PPG and 23rd in Offensive Rating.

Correcting avoidable and "controllable" mistakes will be key in this one, and every game moving forward for that matter.

The Suns' defense will be instrumental in turning this entire thing around, but especially this game. A struggling Magic squad could be just what the doctor ordered. But Orlando is thinking the same thing about the Suns' defense being what they need to get their slumping offense back on track.

Set the tone defensively. Rebound the basketball. Play connected. And most importantly…

Play confident! These stretches can make you second-guess or lose confidence in yourself individually, or as a team. Phoenix needs to do all it can to block out all the noise and get back to focusing on the basics and taking it one game at a time.

Protect your home floor. Get win number two. Build some momentum heading into a tough 2-game stretch against the Rockets and Spurs after this one. The alternative… is not fun to think about.

I've predicted against them in just about every game thus far, so it's time to flip the "hope" switch on and speak their second win into existence.

#2851. Bronny James assigned to G League's Coachella Valley Lakers after playing limited minutes early in third NBA season

Bronny James made the Los Angeles Lakers' 2026-27 opening night roster, securing a spot while his draft classmate, first-round pick Dalton Knecht, was waived.

But after James averaged 2.4 minutes in five appearances amid the Lakers' 7-2 start, the team announced Thursday that it assigned the 2024 second-round pick to the G League's Coachella Valley Lakers.

Fellow guard Cameron Carr was also sent to the franchise's G League affiliate ahead of its Friday night season opener against the Utah Jazz's Salt Lake City Stars.

Bronny James — in his first season in the Lakers' organization without his father, LeBron James — previously spent time in the G League, where his stat lines have often dwarfed those he has authored in the NBA.

Across a combined 25 regular-season games the past two seasons with the then-South Bay Lakers — who have since relocated and rebranded — Bronny James posted 18.4 points, 4.4 assists, 4 rebounds and 1.5 steals per game while shooting 49.6% from the field, including 41.3% from beyond the arc.

James, now 22, was the No. 55 pick when he was drafted out of USC more than two years ago. He appeared in 27 regular-season games with the Lakers as a rookie, making father-son history but struggling mightily at times, particularly early on.

He made noticeable progress in Year 2, taking the court for 42 regular-season games and raising his field-goal percentage from 31.3% (2024-25) to 40.9% (2025-26) and his 3-point percentage from 28.1% (2024-25) to 38.6% (2025-26).

Head coach JJ Redick saw enough from James to include him in his playoff rotation last season, albeit a shorthanded one.

That paved the way for additional excitement and milestones.

Coming into Year 3, the question was: How would James fare without his superstar father on the Lakers (including how would he be treated by staff and teammates in the absence of the future Hall of Famer)?

But James hasn't been given much of an opportunity in the regular season to make any kind of jump. In his sparing minutes, he scored 0.8 points per game. He attempted only three field goals and made two of them.

James could wind up back with the Lakers soon, but his lack of on-court involvement so far isn't promising, in terms of his chances of carving out the long-term 3-and-D role he teased on occasion last season.

As for Knecht, he ended up signing a two-way deal with the New York Knicks, for whom he logged 10 points on 4-of-6 shooting, plus seven rebounds, on Wednesday in a win over the Chicago Bulls.

Bundesliga strugglers Holstein Kiel.

They have also lost all five previous encounters with English clubs, with the most recent meeting ending in a 3-2 home defeat to eventual champions Tottenham Hotspur in the 2024/25 season.

Jean-Philippe Mateta was sent off inside the first 15 minutes when Palace's losing streak extended to three games in the Premier League with a 2-0 defeat at rejuvenated Spurs. After grabbing his first goal of the season in the 2-2 draw at Besiktas on matchday 3, the former Mainz striker will hope to score in the Europa League once again, though.

Fellow former Bundesliga players Daichi Kamada and Chris Richards, who played for Hoffenheim in a couple of loan stints from FC Bayern, are also expected to make the starting lineup. The hosts could get a double boost as Jorgen Strand Larsen and Quinten Timber are hoping to pass late fitness tests.

#2835. Which Cincinnati teams won Nov. 5 girls volleyball regional semis?

The Ohio High School Athletic Association girls volleyball tournament is heating up, with just eight teams remaining in each of the seven divisions after regional semifinal matches concluded on Nov. 5.

Entering the regional semifinals, 15 Cincinnati girls volleyball teams remained alive across five OHSAA divisions. Two defending champions advanced on Nov. 4, with eight games remaining on Nov. 5. After intense battles on the court, eight Greater Cincinnati teams are moving on to the regional championship matches.

Take a look at which teams will battle for a regional championship and the right to play in the state tournament.

Undefeated Seton had already punched its ticket to the regional finals. On Nov. 5, both St. Ursula and Ursuline Academy swept their way through the semifinals and set up a massive showdown in the regional championship.

St. Ursula defeated Olentangy Orange in straight sets, 25-18, 25-19, 25-21, while Ursuline defeated Dublin Coffman in three, 25-19, 25-23, 25-23. The Bulldogs' and Lions' senior classes have grown accustomed to this high level of success: the two teams have now combined for six regional final appearances over the past four seasons.

The upcoming Region 3 championship at Springfield becomes a rubber match in an intense conference rivalry. St. Ursula (17-8) and Ursuline (18-7) split the regular-season series this year, with each team winning in the other's gym. Now, the second- and third-ranked teams in Division I face off at 2 p.m. on Saturday, Nov. 7, with a trip to the state finals on the line.

The Kings Knights (21-3) entered Butler High School on Nov. 5 with one mission: vanquish the memories of a four-set loss to Dublin Scioto in the 2025 regional semifinals.

Behind a dominant attack scheme and an aggressive serve, they took down Harrison (22-3) 25-19, 25-19, 25-15 to advance to Saturday's Division II, Region 8 final.

"I love playing against good competition. We scouted them and knew their strengths and weaknesses and I thought we did a really good job of balancing things out," Kings head coach Amanda Meadows said.

Since their state championship season of 2023, Kings has worn shirts with the phrase, "All gas. No Brakes." During their current 17-match winning streak, they have demonstrated the ability to pull away from teams at any point in the set.

"They did a couple things that we didn't handle as well. We couldn't keep our serve. They serve-receive at a high rate. We didn't serve enough and they served tough," Harrison head coach Phil Deak said.

With the first set tied 10-10, the Knights rattled off four straight points, keeping the Wildcats at arm's length the rest of the way. In the second set, they went to work at the service line. Laura Raines rattled off seven straight points for a 8-4 lead before Kennady Horsley served out the final five points of the set. The third set was a total takeover as they expanded a 3-2 lead to 9-3, eventually gaining an eight-point advantage at 16-8.

As she has done all year, freshman Sammi Wehrman led the way with 23 kills on a .524 hitting percentage. Raines was second in the category with nine terminations, as the Knights hit .398 as a team.

"She's amazing. I know that in games where I struggle, she definitely steps up and vice versa. We balance off each other really well in service," Raines said.

Senior setter Anabelle Seni had 22 assists to go with three blocks while her protege, freshman Dri Naranjo, had 14 assists and 10 digs.

"They are so different and honestly, I've never run a four-two in my life, but when you look at our dynamics, I don't do things the same way," Meadows said. "Dri's strength, in addition to having great hands, is defense. The girl is an amazing defensive player and having reactions like that obviously comes in hand as a setter. Anabelle is a great setter, great leader, but literally the best blocking setter I've ever coached in my life."

Behind seniors Maddie Ward, Lucy Longworth, Kenna Farris and Stella Whitis, the Wildcats tied the program mark for most wins in a season. They started 10-0 with a win over Taylor and took down Wyoming, Cincinnati Hills Christian Academy and Little Miami in the regular season before beating Anderson for a second time to win a district title.

Farris set the program's single-season record for assists along the way.

"They're a special group of girls. Anytime anything happens for the team, it's because they have good attitudes, they work hard, they like being around each other and they're fun kids," Deak said. "Being in the gym never seemed like it was work. It seemed like it was an opportunity to be together."

The DII, Region 8 final is 2 p.m. Nov. 7 at Butler High School.

The Cougars only needed one win for a district title and a rematch with Anthony Wayne, who swept MND in the 2025 DII state semifinals.

The match that unfolded was truly worthy of a state championship state as the Generals took down the Cougars 25-19, 17-25, 25-19, 22-25, 22-20.

"I thought they did a great job of adjusting against us and it's kind of a game of readjusting to everything," MND head coach Dani Szczepanski said. "It was great volleyball to watch and I could not ask for a better game to be a part of."

The first set was a sign of things to come as Wayne took an early lead at 13-9, but the Cougars just kept hanging around behind attacks from Leah Seeger and Dylan Kozma.

MND responded in the second set, taking advantage of the Generals' deep back line with short, diving serves and an attack that constantly found the floor in zone six.

While the Cougars didn't win the third set, they mounted a comeback from 10 points down to get within four points at 23-19. Their bugaboo in the set was too many attacks that sailed long, a problem that also plagued them in the first stanza. Anthony Wayne's front line of Leah Ruhe, Charley Gardner, Olive Baker and Addelyn O'Shea also feasted on any ball within reach.

"It was a very up-and-down match for us. When we were really high, we were really high. Then when we were really low, we let them get really high, which made us go lower. Our word for the year was 'resilient,' and I thought our girls did a great job of being that during the game," Szczepanski said.

The final two sets took a turn for the dramatic, the tightest two of the match by far. Down 4-1 early in the fourth, MND worked its way back to tie it at 8-8. After the Generals surged to a 15-11 lead, kills from Reagin Rozier, Aubrey McDaniel and Seeger, plus two Grace Shafer aces, tied the match at 17-17. It would remain tied at every point going forward until a Rozier block, a Wayne attack error and a kill from McDaniel sent it to a dramatic fifth set.

After 10 points, it appeared the Cougars had seized all the momentum as two kills and a block from Kendall Hubbard propelled them to a 7-3 lead. Anthony Wayne responded with a 9-2 run out of a timeout to take a 12-9 advantage. After Szczepanski burned her final timeout, kills from Seeger, Kozma and McDaniel gave MND a 13-12 lead.

From there, neither team had a two-point advantage until the final margin. Both teams had four match points, the first three on the Cougars' side, then four of the next five in favor of the Generals.

With the deciding set tied at 20, MND committed an attack error before Hubbard was called for lift to bring the marathon to its finish line.

"We didn't have the best record in the season, had some adversity to fight through and had some injuries. We were trying to find our footing a lot and I feel like that's how we were tonight. When we were looking for our footing, it was hard to find, but once we found it, we were climbing super high," Szczepanski said.

The Cougars close the season at 12-11 and graduate six seniors: Hubbard, Shafer, Seeger, McDaniel, Kozma and Cami Reisert.

"We are going to miss them greatly, not only from a volleyball aspect but from a leadership aspect and who they are as human beings. That's going to be a big loss for our program but I'm really proud of them. They stepped up in many different ways this year," Szczepanski said.

After two sets, Fenwick found its back against the wall. Wyoming had won each by narrow margins, but time was running out on Fenwick's chances for a third consecutive trip to the regional finals. The Falcons responded in force, completing the reverse sweep with a 23-25, 22-25, 25-20, 25-20, 15-12 victory against the Cowboys.

Wyoming becomes just the second team to take Fenwick to five sets, with McNicholas doing so in the regular season. The Cowboys' season comes to an end at 18-6. Wyoming will graduate five seniors: Sarah Mouch, Athina Rickert, Phoebe Alvis, Emily Streets and Morgan Thomas.

Taylor blasted Johnstown in the opening set, and the Yellowjackets never looked back. Taylor advances to the regional finals with a 25-11, 25-18, 25-9 win over the Johnnies in the semifinals. The Yellowjackets have never reached the state finals before and still face a final significant hurdle against Greater Catholic League-Coed co-champion Fenwick.

Fenwick (25-2) takes on Taylor (24-2) in the Division V, Region 15 championship game at Lakota East. The match will be played at 2 p.m. on Saturday, Nov. 7.

Madeira is one game away from ending a 45-year state tournament drought after a 25-19, 25-21, 25-15 sweep against Arcanum. The Amazons haven't reached the Final Four since 1981, when they finished as state runners-up. The only thing standing in their way is a revenge match against Versailles, which handed Madeira a defeat in the 2025 district championship match. Versailles eliminated Milford Center Fairbanks in three sets to set up the grudge match.

Madeira (20-6) will face Versailles (20-6) in the regional championship at 2 p.m. on Saturday, Nov. 7, at Kettering Fairmont.
