#2834. Warriors vs. Heat injury report: Gui Santos remains out

After a slow start to the season, the Golden State Warriors are officially rolling. They've won five games in a row — something they didn't do at any point last year — and have a prime chance to make it six in a row tonight, when they welcome in old friend Klay Thompson and the Miami Heat. The Heat are just 3-5 on the year, and not only are they playing the second game of a back-to-back (while the Dubs are coming off two straight off days), but their game last night went to overtime. So the Warriors should be comfortably favored here, but needless to say, nothing is ever easy against Giannis Antetokounmpo, Bam Adebayo, and Erik Spoelstra.

The Warriors, as is the case in almost every game they play, are less healthy than their opponent, though. Here's the full injury report for both teams.

Santos will miss his second straight game as the Warriors try to get him healthy. When he has played this year, he hasn't appeared to be at full health, so it looks like Golden State will give him a chunk of time off to get him fully healthy before reintegrating him into the lineup.

Out — Jimmy Butler III (right ACL repair surgery)Out — Moses Moody (left patellar tendon repair surgery)Out — Kristaps Porziņģis (not with team)

Same old, same old with this trio. Porziņģis may not be getting paid, but he technically still is a member of the roster, and will remain on the injury report for as long as that's the case.

Available — Giannis Antetokounmpo (right wrist sprain)

The only player on Miami's injury report is someone who will be playing, so they're at full strength. In his first year in South Beach, Antetokounmpo is putting up his usual absurd stats, as he's averaging 28.3 points, 12.5 rebounds, 5.8 assists, 1.0 steals, and 1.1 blocks per game.

Enjoy the game, everyone! It tips off at 7:00 p.m. PT on NBA TV and NBC Sports Bay Area.

#2832. Joe Brady on cutting S C.J. Gardner-Johnson: "It was the best interests of the football team"

On the day the NFL suspended former Bills safety C.J. Gardner-Johnson for spitting on an opponent, Buffalo coach Joe Brady said the team would not be cutting Gardner-Johnson. After the game that Gardner-Johnson missed, the Bills cut him.

Meeting with reporters on Thursday, Brady explained the reversal.

“There was a lot to evaluate,” Brady said. “You know, we got together and figured out, ‘Hey, this is what we felt like this was the best thing to do for the Buffalo Bills.’ I have so much love for [C.J.]. Like, I really do. I loved getting to coach him. I loved being around him. I loved — I did love the edge, and his personality allowing him to be him, right?, and as a person. So that didn’t make the decision easy at all.

“But we also have to be an organization that, you know, it’s unfortunate obviously what happened, you know? But it also opened a door and an opportunity for guys the other day, and that was a part of evaluation. And seeing how those guys kind of played, and you know with a lot of the roster moves. And, again, this wasn’t — we didn’t come out of the game post Vegas and say, ‘This is what we were going to do. We’re just gonna do this the following Monday.’ We were, you know, the plan was him being here and we were going to evaluate it after the game in Baltimore. And we felt like with some of the moving parts we had to do it was the best interests of the football team.”

Gardner-Johnson’s suspension opened the door for Damar Hamlin to start the game against the Ravens. And Hamlin apparently did well enough to help the Bills realize that, all things considered, the “best interests” of the Bills meant the Bills were better off without the “high maintenance” Gardner-Johnson.

#2831. Minkah Fitzpatrick clears air on ‘Jets being Jets’ controversy during losing streak

There hasn't been much good news around the Jets this season, and their misery was amplified after Sunday's loss to the Raiders when veteran safety Minkah Fitzpatrick appeared to have said the team's defensive mistakes were "Jets being Jets."

But Fitzpatrick reiterated Thursday he instead said it was the "Jets beating Jets," referring to self-inflicted wounds due to penalties and miscues.

For a team that entered the season with playoff aspirations and enters Sunday's game in Kansas City just 2-6, having lost six of seven and three in a row, it was an unwanted distraction that Fitzpatrick said didn't impact the mood of the locker room.

"It hasn't really caused any stir within this locker room,'' Fitzpatrick said. "That's the most important thing. Everyone in this locker room knew what I was talking about. If you look at the context of what I said, if you look at what I've been saying all season, we've been addressing 'Jets not beating Jets' or 'Jets beating Jets.' Everyone in this locker room knows exactly what I said, what I was trying to communicate, and in my opinion, that's all that matters."

That, and maybe getting a win for the first time since they beat the Browns on Oct. 11.

Sunday, they'll visit a Chiefs team that has dropped its past two after opening the year with five consecutive wins.

"I know what I said,'' Fitzpatrick said. "It doesn't matter. We're playing Kansas City this week. … This locker room needs to focus on winning right now — and not something that I might have not said or something I might have done. Our focus and objective now is to win and not focus on should've, would've, could've."

Jets safety Minkah Fitzpatrick speaks to the media after practice in Florham Park, N.J., Thursday, Nov. 5, 2026. Bill Kostroun for NY Post

Fitzpatrick said the controversy impacted him more because it had the potential to distract the team from what they have to do on the field to try to salvage something of what's been another ugly season.

"It more so bothered me in the moment, when we're a team that's fighting to win and we're talking about stuff that doesn't really matter, stuff that doesn't factor into victory,'' Fitzpatrick said.

Garrett Wilson, who's been in concussion protocol, did not practice again Thursday. If he can't go Sunday in Kansas City, the offense will look considerably different — even if he has occasionally lacked targets.

"In one respect, you can't replace Garrett Wilson,'' offensive coordinator Frank Reich said. "He's a unique player. But on the other hand, we're not afraid. We saw the game [Adonai Mitchell] had. We saw the game [Omar Cooper Jr.] had. We have a lot of confidence in our guys. They're different players, so maybe you do slightly different things. If Garrett's up, we'll do Garrett things. If he's not, we'll do [Mitchell] things or [Cooper] things. You have to play to your players' strengths."

David Bailey was a full participant Thursday after being limited Wednesday with a groin injury.

After the defense was torched by Las Vegas tight end Brock Bowers on Sunday, defensive coordinator Brian Duker was asked Thursday what he expected from Kansas City and Travis Kelce.

"I wouldn't anticipate reading their minds on that,'' Duker said, before adding of Kelce, "He's probably used a little differently [than Bowers]. Obviously, both are very good, but they're probably used a little differently."

#2829. What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique

What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

They're a team of superstars that don't play superstar basketball.

That's the beauty of this 76ers team. They play a brand of unselfish team-first basketball you rarely see from a team loaded with stars.

LeBron James is one of the two or three greatest players in NBA history, a four-time MVP, a four-time NBA champion, a 1st-ballot Hall of Famer.

Joel Embiid is a seven-time all-star, a former MVP, an Olympic gold medalist and a likely Hall of Famer.

Jaylen Brown is a five-time all-star, an NBA champion, a career 20 points-per-game scorer.

Tyrese Maxey is a two-time all-star, one of only two NBA guards averaging 27.0 points per game over the last four years.

Four certified megastars on one team. And somehow they've figured out how to leave their egos at the front door and play smart, tough, disciplined team basketball, the style of ball you just never see on these superstar-laden teams.

On offense, they patiently work the ball around, always looking for the open man or a positive matchup. You've got this bunch of big-name, high-priced All-Stars who are just as happy to pass the ball as shoot it. That never happens.

The 76ers are sixth in the NBA in assists, and they're one of only four teams with twice as many assists as turnovers. Smart, unselfish, patient, disciplined.

When an all-timer like LeBron averages 7.3 assists per game that speaks volumes. LeBron is fifth in the NBA in assists per game among forwards, which is wild. You can see the joy on his face when one of his passes leads to a basket. When one of the greatest players in history has that mentality, it rubs off on everyone around him.

Embiid hasn't really got going yet on offense and still takes too many 3's, but he's also embraced the team-first mentality, and his 4.8 assists per game are more than one full assist above his career average and 4th-most in the NBA among centers or forward-centers. And still one of the most dominating defensive players in the league.

Brown is averaging 27.0 points, which you expect, but he's also averaging 4.3 assists per game, significantly above his career average of 3.8 coming into the season. He's fit in so well so fast you forget its his first season here.

And what can you even say about Maxey? Just a beast. He's averaging nearly 30 points per game but never wastes a possession, always makes the right decision with the ball in his hands and is still finding time to average 4.3 assists per game.

With James, Embiid, Brown and Maxey, the 76ers are the only NBA team with four guys averaging at least 16 points and 4 assists per game. Only the Pacers, Magic and Raptors have three.

But where we're really seeing this group playing out of their minds is on the defensive end, where this bunch of all-stars, including two newcomers, has been astounding.

The 76ers have the 2nd-best defensive efficiency rating in the league, behind only the Pistons, who they play Wednesday night, and they're allowing the 2nd-fewest points in the league, also behind Detroit. Last year, they allowed 123 points per game through eight games. This year they're at 111.

They're holding teams to 43 percent shooting from the field, 3rd-best in the league and their lowest since 2011-12. Their 9.3 steals per game are 6th-most.

All five starters are among the top 25 players in the league in defensive rating – James is fourth, Embiid 11th, Edgecombe 15th, Maxey 22nd and Brown 23rd. How is that even possible?

It's rare to see superstar-caliber guys play as hard on the defensive end as the offensive end. Everybody on this team embraces it. They hustle, they work, they communicate and most importantly they commit to it.

The biggest concern when the 76ers built this dream team was that it would be a team of individuals out for themselves, but the reality has been the opposite.

In a very short period of time, they've learned how to play together. How to play for each other.

And we haven't even mentioned V.J. Edgecomb, who at 21 years old has figured out how to assimilate into this loaded lineup, never force anything, wait for his opportunities and make huge shots when he gets his turn. Edgecombe is at 12.7 points and 3.0 assists per game, and his numbers are understandably down a bit from last year, but if that bothers him it sure doesn't show.

And the role players – Dean Wade, Anfernee Simons, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, Jabari Walker – have each adopted the same ego-free style of basketball, supporting the starters however they need to.

Nick Nurse deserves a ton of credit for getting this bunch to play this brand of hoops. It's not easy to get any NBA player to put team goals ahead of individual goals. But he's got Hall of Famers and all-stars doing it and having a blast along the way. They all seem to love playing basketball the right way and they seem to love playing together.

The 76ers are 7-1 and looking like a real threat to win the East, and they're doing it by playing the game the right way.

By committing to each other and not themselves.

#2828. Which Cincinnati teams won Nov. 5 girls volleyball regional semis?

The Ohio High School Athletic Association girls volleyball tournament is heating up, with just eight teams remaining in each of the seven divisions after regional semifinal matches concluded on Nov. 5.

Entering the regional semifinals, 15 Cincinnati girls volleyball teams remained alive across five OHSAA divisions. Two defending champions advanced on Nov. 4, with eight games remaining on Nov. 5. After intense battles on the court, eight Greater Cincinnati teams are moving on to the regional championship matches.

Take a look at which teams will battle for a regional championship and the right to play in the state tournament.

Undefeated Seton had already punched its ticket to the regional finals. On Nov. 5, both St. Ursula and Ursuline Academy swept their way through the semifinals and set up a massive showdown in the regional championship.

St. Ursula defeated Olentangy Orange in straight sets, 25-18, 25-19, 25-21, while Ursuline defeated Dublin Coffman in three, 25-19, 25-23, 25-23. The Bulldogs' and Lions' senior classes have grown accustomed to this high level of success: the two teams have now combined for six regional final appearances over the past four seasons.

The upcoming Region 3 championship at Springfield becomes a rubber match in an intense conference rivalry. St. Ursula (17-8) and Ursuline (18-7) split the regular-season series this year, with each team winning in the other's gym. Now, the second- and third-ranked teams in Division I face off at 2 p.m. on Saturday, Nov. 7, with a trip to the state finals on the line.

The Kings Knights (21-3) entered Butler High School on Nov. 5 with one mission: vanquish the memories of a four-set loss to Dublin Scioto in the 2025 regional semifinals.

Behind a dominant attack scheme and an aggressive serve, they took down Harrison (22-3) 25-19, 25-19, 25-15 to advance to Saturday's Division II, Region 8 final.

"I love playing against good competition. We scouted them and knew their strengths and weaknesses and I thought we did a really good job of balancing things out," Kings head coach Amanda Meadows said.

Since their state championship season of 2023, Kings has worn shirts with the phrase, "All gas. No Brakes." During their current 17-match winning streak, they have demonstrated the ability to pull away from teams at any point in the set.

"They did a couple things that we didn't handle as well. We couldn't keep our serve. They serve-receive at a high rate. We didn't serve enough and they served tough," Harrison head coach Phil Deak said.

With the first set tied 10-10, the Knights rattled off four straight points, keeping the Wildcats at arm's length the rest of the way. In the second set, they went to work at the service line. Laura Raines rattled off seven straight points for a 8-4 lead before Kennady Horsley served out the final five points of the set. The third set was a total takeover as they expanded a 3-2 lead to 9-3, eventually gaining an eight-point advantage at 16-8.

As she has done all year, freshman Sammi Wehrman led the way with 23 kills on a .524 hitting percentage. Raines was second in the category with nine terminations, as the Knights hit .398 as a team.

"She's amazing. I know that in games where I struggle, she definitely steps up and vice versa. We balance off each other really well in service," Raines said.

Senior setter Anabelle Seni had 22 assists to go with three blocks while her protege, freshman Dri Naranjo, had 14 assists and 10 digs.

"They are so different and honestly, I've never run a four-two in my life, but when you look at our dynamics, I don't do things the same way," Meadows said. "Dri's strength, in addition to having great hands, is defense. The girl is an amazing defensive player and having reactions like that obviously comes in hand as a setter. Anabelle is a great setter, great leader, but literally the best blocking setter I've ever coached in my life."

Behind seniors Maddie Ward, Lucy Longworth, Kenna Farris and Stella Whitis, the Wildcats tied the program mark for most wins in a season. They started 10-0 with a win over Taylor and took down Wyoming, Cincinnati Hills Christian Academy and Little Miami in the regular season before beating Anderson for a second time to win a district title.

Farris set the program's single-season record for assists along the way.

"They're a special group of girls. Anytime anything happens for the team, it's because they have good attitudes, they work hard, they like being around each other and they're fun kids," Deak said. "Being in the gym never seemed like it was work. It seemed like it was an opportunity to be together."

The DII, Region 8 final is 2 p.m. Nov. 7 at Butler High School.

The Cougars only needed one win for a district title and a rematch with Anthony Wayne, who swept MND in the 2025 DII state semifinals.

The match that unfolded was truly worthy of a state championship state as the Generals took down the Cougars 25-19, 17-25, 25-19, 22-25, 22-20.

"I thought they did a great job of adjusting against us and it's kind of a game of readjusting to everything," MND head coach Dani Szczepanski said. "It was great volleyball to watch and I could not ask for a better game to be a part of."

The first set was a sign of things to come as Wayne took an early lead at 13-9, but the Cougars just kept hanging around behind attacks from Leah Seeger and Dylan Kozma.

MND responded in the second set, taking advantage of the Generals' deep back line with short, diving serves and an attack that constantly found the floor in zone six.

While the Cougars didn't win the third set, they mounted a comeback from 10 points down to get within four points at 23-19. Their bugaboo in the set was too many attacks that sailed long, a problem that also plagued them in the first stanza. Anthony Wayne's front line of Leah Ruhe, Charley Gardner, Olive Baker and Addelyn O'Shea also feasted on any ball within reach.

"It was a very up-and-down match for us. When we were really high, we were really high. Then when we were really low, we let them get really high, which made us go lower. Our word for the year was 'resilient,' and I thought our girls did a great job of being that during the game," Szczepanski said.

The final two sets took a turn for the dramatic, the tightest two of the match by far. Down 4-1 early in the fourth, MND worked its way back to tie it at 8-8. After the Generals surged to a 15-11 lead, kills from Reagin Rozier, Aubrey McDaniel and Seeger, plus two Grace Shafer aces, tied the match at 17-17. It would remain tied at every point going forward until a Rozier block, a Wayne attack error and a kill from McDaniel sent it to a dramatic fifth set.

After 10 points, it appeared the Cougars had seized all the momentum as two kills and a block from Kendall Hubbard propelled them to a 7-3 lead. Anthony Wayne responded with a 9-2 run out of a timeout to take a 12-9 advantage. After Szczepanski burned her final timeout, kills from Seeger, Kozma and McDaniel gave MND a 13-12 lead.

From there, neither team had a two-point advantage until the final margin. Both teams had four match points, the first three on the Cougars' side, then four of the next five in favor of the Generals.

With the deciding set tied at 20, MND committed an attack error before Hubbard was called for lift to bring the marathon to its finish line.

"We didn't have the best record in the season, had some adversity to fight through and had some injuries. We were trying to find our footing a lot and I feel like that's how we were tonight. When we were looking for our footing, it was hard to find, but once we found it, we were climbing super high," Szczepanski said.

The Cougars close the season at 12-11 and graduate six seniors: Hubbard, Shafer, Seeger, McDaniel, Kozma and Cami Reisert.

"We are going to miss them greatly, not only from a volleyball aspect but from a leadership aspect and who they are as human beings. That's going to be a big loss for our program but I'm really proud of them. They stepped up in many different ways this year," Szczepanski said.

After two sets, Fenwick found its back against the wall. Wyoming had won each by narrow margins, but time was running out on Fenwick's chances for a third consecutive trip to the regional finals. The Falcons responded in force, completing the reverse sweep with a 23-25, 22-25, 25-20, 25-20, 15-12 victory against the Cowboys.

Wyoming becomes just the second team to take Fenwick to five sets, with McNicholas doing so in the regular season. The Cowboys' season comes to an end at 18-6. Wyoming will graduate five seniors: Sarah Mouch, Athina Rickert, Phoebe Alvis, Emily Streets and Morgan Thomas.

Taylor blasted Johnstown in the opening set, and the Yellowjackets never looked back. Taylor advances to the regional finals with a 25-11, 25-18, 25-9 win over the Johnnies in the semifinals. The Yellowjackets have never reached the state finals before and still face a final significant hurdle against Greater Catholic League-Coed co-champion Fenwick.

Fenwick (25-2) takes on Taylor (24-2) in the Division V, Region 15 championship game at Lakota East. The match will be played at 2 p.m. on Saturday, Nov. 7.

Madeira is one game away from ending a 45-year state tournament drought after a 25-19, 25-21, 25-15 sweep against Arcanum. The Amazons haven't reached the Final Four since 1981, when they finished as state runners-up. The only thing standing in their way is a revenge match against Versailles, which handed Madeira a defeat in the 2025 district championship match. Versailles eliminated Milford Center Fairbanks in three sets to set up the grudge match.

Madeira (20-6) will face Versailles (20-6) in the regional championship at 2 p.m. on Saturday, Nov. 7, at Kettering Fairmont.

This article originally appeared on Cincinnati Enquirer: Which Cincinnati teams won Nov. 5 girls volleyball regional semis?

#2825. Bayer Leverkusen vs. Olympique de Marseille preview: Team news and predicted lineups

Bayer Leverkusen will hope to continue their winning start in Europe when they host Marseille on Thursday. The French club lost all the first three games in the competition, although their results improved in the league since the start of last month.

In contrast, Carles Martinez's side lost back-to-back Bundesliga games by conceding seven goals to Hoffenheim and Stuttgart. Leverkusen's inconsistent league form saw them dropped to sixth place, although they're only five points behind FC Bayern and Borussia Dortmund.

Marseille won four successive Ligue 1 matches after their narrow Le Classique defeat to the all-conquering PSG side. This will be the first competitive fixture between the clubs, but they met multiple times in friendlies, including Leverkusen's win at Velodrome three years ago.

Leverkusen have won seven of their 15 meetings against French clubs, but they lost 7-2 to PSG at BayArena in last season's Champions League campaign. This is the first time they face a team from Europe's top-five leagues in the league phase of the Europa League this term.

The last time Marseille faced a German club they lost to Eintracht Frankfurt home and away in the group stage of the 2022/23 Champions League. Bruno Genesio's side failed to score against Olympiacos and Sturm Graz after starting their European campaign with a heavy defeat at Besiktas.

Summer signings Moussa Diaby and Guela Doue remain on the sidelines as Martinez could field a strong side because of several long-term absentees. Janis Blaswich will hope to keep his place between the sticks after replacing Mark Flekken for the recent European game against OFI Crete.

Loic Bade and Nathan Tella will hope to make their returns to the matchday squad for Leverkusen. It'll be a special game for Argentine international Facundo Medina as he faces the club he left in the summer.

Former OM captain Pierre-Emile Højbjerg and Amine Harit had previous Bundesliga spells. Experienced defender Emerson is a doubt for Marseille after his early substitution against Martinez’s former club Toulouse on Sunday.

#2824. No. 11 UCLA's defense leads the way in 78-42 victory over UC Irvine

The No. 11 UCLA Bruins looked a bit sharper in their second game of the season, knocking off UC Irvine 78-42 on Thursday to move to 2-0 on the young season.

After battling in the first half in the season opener against Lehigh, the Bruins got off to a much better start this time around. UCLA led 23-8 after the first quarter, as the team didn't allow the lesser-talented Anteaters to stick around.

UCLA finished strong too, pitching a shutout in the fourth quarter. The Bruins didn't allow a single fourth quarter point, which is a good sign for a new-look Cori Close defense that no longer has Lauren Betts roaming the paint. Four different Bruins recorded a block, as rim protection will likely be a group effort this season.

KK Bransford led UCLA with four steals and freshman Somto Okafor looks like a very promising defender on the wing for UCLA. Okafor notched three steals in 23 minutes off the bench on Thursday. Okafor grabbed three steals in the opener on Monday beginning her UCLA career with back-to-back three steal games.

UCLA had four scorers in double-figures but it was sophomore forward Sienna Betts leading the team with 17 points on 8-10 shooting in just 21 minutes. Junior guard Elina Aarnisalo added 14 points with five rebounds and five assists but did turn the ball over six times.

Timea Gardiner continues to look strong in her return to action. Gardiner missed all of last season but the forward scored 12 points, grabbed five rebounds and knocked down a pair of threes. The three point line was still a battle for UCLA though, shooting 25% on 24 three point attempts.

It was an improved showing for the national champions, with UCLA displaying an elite defense that will be crucial to their success this season. UCLA will have a full week before their next game, as they'll host Arizona next Thursday.

#2823. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2822. Rashee Rice files for bankruptcy, citing more than $3.45 million in debt

Chiefs receiver Rashee Rice is in the final year of his four-year, $6.45 million rookie contract. He nevertheless has far greater liabilities than assets. And he’s looking to wipe the slate clean as to at least some of them.

Via ESPN, Rice has filed for federal bankruptcy protection.

The filing cites more than $3.45 million in debts, with $2.1 million of it tied to multiple plaintiffs in the accident caused by his street-racing incident in early 2024.

Rice has yet to get a second contract. He’s due to become a free agent in March, and he has played well enough to get signed, by the Chiefs or someone else.

By then, Rice should have more than enough money to pay all of his debts without relying on the ability to activate Chapter 7 of the bankruptcy laws to discharge some or many of them for cents on the dollar.

That could be the first argument made by his creditors. Given Rice’s earning potential, it’s just a matter of time before he has the money needed to pay all of his bills and settle all of his debts.

#2819. After Being Released By The Senators, Prescott's Ben Hutton Lands A New NHL Job

Prescott's Ben Hutton won't be playing for his hometown Senators this season, but the veteran defenceman has found himself another NHL job.

The New York Islanders announced Thursday they've signed the 33-year-old to a one-year deal, just three days after bringing him in on a professional tryout.

Hutton, who was born in Brockville and raised in Prescott, spent part of September trying to earn a job with the Senators. He signed a PTO with the club on September 17, but after appearing in two preseason games, Hutton was released on September 29.

Ben Hutton talks about how he's been staying prepared for another NHL opportunity over the past month (New York Islanders YouTube).

It was a disappointing outcome for the local product, who felt like he had a solid 2025-26 season with the Vegas Golden Knights.

Hutton appeared in 55 games, putting up six goals and 15 points. His goal total was a career high, and he also appeared in seven playoff games as the Knights made it to the Cup Final.

Despite all that, Hutton was unable to secure a contract.

"It's been a whirlwind," Hutton told reporters this week. "Never really experienced something like this. Been in the league for a little time now, and thought I had a good year. Nothing came of it."

Turns out the Sens could have used him this season, at least in the early going. At one point last month, the Sens were so banged up on the blue line, they dressed a number of defensemen (Carter Yakemchuk, Cameron Crotty, Hoyt Stanley, Jorian Donovan) who all had almost no NHL experience.

The Islanders have their own share of injuries. Ryan Pulock suffered an upper-body injury expected to keep him out for two to four weeks. The Islanders are also without Alexander Romanov, probably for the season, after he suffered a torn ACL in October. So they're pleased that a player like Hutton was available at this stage.

Islanders head coach Pete DeBoer has a pretty good idea what the team is getting. He was Hutton's head coach in Vegas, though only for one year. The following season, under Bruce Cassidy, Hutton won a Cup with the Knights.

Although things didn't work out with the Senators, it's nice to see the likeable local veteran get another opportunity to show he can still contribute at the NHL level.

#2816. Patriots rooting guide for NFL Week 9

And just like that it's November. In football terms, this means the real season is about to start. For the New England Patriots, it starts with a game against the Green Bay Packers at 4:25 p.m. ET on Sunday.

There are 14 other contests on the menu this weekend, however, so let's find out who to root for in the non-Patriots games.

Jacksonville Jaguars (5-2) at Baltimore Ravens (4-4): Go Ravens! Baltimore is down to its third-string quarterback, and even he is banged up. It would take a lot for them to win, but that's precisely what we are rooting for on Thursday night. | Prime Video

Cincinnati Bengals (5-2) at Atlanta Falcons (3-5): Go Falcons! It's your classic AFC-vs.-NFC matchups that naturally has us rooting for the NFC side. | NFL Network

Denver Broncos (6-2) at Carolina Panthers (3-4): Go Panthers! Same reason as above. We are picking the NFC team over the AFC team. | CBS

Dallas Cowboys (3-5) at Indianapolis Colts (2-6): Go Cowboys! The Colts' season is spiritually over already, and they are headed into a major rebuilding process. Still, as long as there is a theoretical chance they could go on a run, it's better to be save than sorry. | FOX

New York Jets (2-6) at Kansas City Chiefs (5-2): Go Jets! The Patriots have a major advantage over their division rivals already, both in total standings and head-to-head, so rooting for them over the Chiefs makes the most sense with the playoff picture in mind. | CBS

Detroit Lions (5-2) at Miami Dolphins (1-6): Go Lions! The Patriots' Week 10 opponent visiting their Week 8 opponent has us rooting for the NFC team. We are not quite yet in "let's mess up their draft position territory" yet, but we will be getting there soon one would imagine. | CBS

Cleveland Browns (5-3) at New Orleans Saints (2-5): Go Saints! Another clear-as-day pick that has us go with the NFC team over the AFC team. | CBS

New York Giants (4-2-1) at Philadelphia Eagles (2-6): Go good game! This matters not from a Patriots point of view. | FOX

Los Angeles Rams (4-4) at Washington Commanders (2-4-1): Go good game! This game also has no direct impact on New England. | FOX

Houston Texans (2-5) at Los Angeles Chargers (0-7): Go Chargers! There are two reasons to be rooting for the Chargers' first win of the season: to keep the Texans at two wins and to help New England's sixth-round draft pick that was acquired in the Marte Mapu trade. A win-win. | CBS

Arizona Cardinals (3-5) at Seattle Seahawks (4-4): Go Seahawks! This pick comes down to the Patriots' strength of schedule tiebreaker. | FOX

Las Vegas Raiders (4-4) at San Francisco 49ers (6-1): Go 49ers! While this would hurt said tiebreaker, we are rooting for the NFC over the AFC team here. | FOX

Tampa Bay Buccaneers (3-5) at Chicago Bears (6-2): Go Bears! Once again we are back at strength of schedule. | NBC

Minnesota Vikings (6-1) at Buffalo Bills (6-1): Go Vikings! In a week of many easy picks, this might be the easiest. If the Patriots want to defend their AFC East crown, they need the Bills to lose some non-head-to-head games. This looks like a potential candidate, despite the Vikings' high probability of being a paper tiger at the end of the day. | ESPN/ABC

#2815. NFL trade deadline: 49ers GM explains Mac Jones trade price

The San Francisco 49ers aren't actively looking to move backup quarterback Mac Jones, and general manager John Lynch made it clear that the team would need a significant offer to consider trading him.

Jones has generated trade speculation throughout the offseason and, with quarterback-needy teams such as the Minnesota Vikings and Philadelphia Eagles potentially looking for options, could be a target ahead of the Nov. 10 deadline.

However, the 49ers have consistently shown little interest in moving Jones without receiving substantial compensation. Appearing on KNBR, Lynch reiterated that San Francisco is comfortable keeping its backup quarterback.

"We'd have to be really motivated to move Mac, a situation where it makes too much sense," Lynch said. "But we're very comfortable with keeping Mac."

That stance makes sense considering what Jones provided the 49ers last season. He went 5-3 as a starter while Brock Purdy dealt with injuries, helping San Francisco reach the playoffs despite significant roster attrition.

Jones proved to be a capable replacement for Purdy, giving the 49ers valuable insurance at the most important position on the field. Reports have indicated that San Francisco would want at least a second-round pick to consider moving him.

With the trade deadline approaching, Lynch's comments suggest that hasn't changed. San Francisco would need an offer it simply couldn't refuse to part with Jones; otherwise, the team appears content to keep one of the NFL's more capable backup quarterbacks behind Purdy.

#2813. Observations after Sixers beat Bulls to reach East-leading 7-1

Observations after Sixers beat Bulls to reach East-leading 7-1 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers kept up their hot start to the season Monday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, reaching 7-1 with a 126-107 win against the Bulls.

All five of the Sixers' starters scored in double figures. Tyrese Maxey posted 26 points on 10-for-15 shooting and 11 assists. Jaylen Brown tallied a season-best 25 points and 11 rebounds.

VJ Edgecombe's 21 points were a new season high. LeBron James recorded 15 points, nine assists and seven boards.

Bulls rookie Caleb Wilson had 24 points and seven rebounds.

Joel Embiid (15 points, eight rebounds) had been listed as questionable with an official designation of "right foot plantar fascia injury management." He sat out Saturday on the second night of a back-to-back for the Sixers' win over the Hornets.

"Plantar is a little bit of a come and go type of thing," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse told reporters pregame. "They're just trying to manage it. It's very painful, if you've ever had it. It can kind of come and go out of nowhere. So we're just trying to manage the pain with that, be as smart as we can."

Nurse told reporters that Dominick Barlow is set to see a specialist Tuesday about the back injury that's sidelined him to start the season.

"He's been on court a little bit … not a ton of moving around," Nurse said. "I think they want to get to tomorrow's appointment. Hopefully, it'll be positive and we can get into his workouts a little bit."

The Sixers will begin a three-game road trip Wednesday when they visit the Pistons. Here are observations on their victory Monday:

After Embiid blitzed a Bulls pick-and-roll, Maxey picked off Matas Buzelis' hurried pass to get the Sixers rolling in transition and James jammed home the team's first field goal.

Edgecombe got a slam soon after James grabbed a defensive rebound and the Sixers again pushed the pace. The Sixers scored six quick fast-break points, leading the Bulls to burn an early timeout, and James' long-distance feeds were a major asset.

"If you're open, he's going to pass it," Edgecombe said of James. "The main thing is just being ready. Tonight I had to run and go get 'em. If you're open and he sees you, he's going to pass the ball."

Edgecombe had a dunk-heavy first quarter. He threw down his second when Dean Wade nabbed a steal and dropped off a typically selfless dish to the Sixers' explosive second-year guard, whose slam put the team up 22-13.

The Sixers have an abundance of dangerous options in the open floor, Edgecombe included. As he's already shown with his continued clutch shotmaking, the 21-year-old won't become anything resembling a non-factor offensively this year as a byproduct of the Sixers' enhanced star power.

Over the team's first seven games, Edgecombe had averaged 11.3 points. He scored 11 in the first period on 5-for-5 shooting.

Adem Bona missed a dunk early in the second quarter, which stung a bit more when Jalen Smith drilled a three-pointer seconds later.

Bona remained the Sixers' main backup center. He had a poor first-half stint overall amid a largely negative start to his third season. The 23-year-old big man played well in the Sixers' victory over the Bucks, posting six points, 13 rebounds and three blocks. Outside of that, his season has been sparse on bright moments so far. It's reasonable to wonder when Nurse might turn to alternatives like Jabari Walker, who played a good game Saturday in Charlotte, or try James again at the five.

The Bulls outscored the Sixers by eight points in Bona's first run, cutting their derficit to 40-38. Chicago went in front with a three by former Sixer Buddy Hield.

The Sixers regained the advantage late in the second quarter. Maxey and James each were excellent as passers in the first half, combining for 14 assists and three turnovers. James fired the ball to Wade for a corner three that gave the Sixers a 60-52 lead.

Following a third-quarter Embiid fadeaway jumper and two James lay-ins, the Sixers were up 17 points.

The Bulls cut the Sixers' lead down to 11 points by the start of the fourth quarter. With Bona still struggling and on four fouls, Wade began the fourth at center. Bona then briefly spelled Wade before being whistled for his fifth foul and taking a seat. Embiid ultimately wasn't needed for any of the fourth period and only logged 25 minutes.

Embiid said postgame that he was not available to return to the action in the fourth and has been feeling his plantar fasciitis on the court. The seven-time All-Star's health is clearly not close to 100 percent.

"I was on a minutes restriction," he said. "(The foot) has been going like that for the past few weeks. It's painful. Just got to manage it.

"If it flares up pretty bad, got to take a step back. We've been able to manage it, but it has its ups and downs."

The Sixers' lead got as low as eight points, but Chicago did not shoot well enough from three-point range (26.3 percent) or have sufficient firepower to sustain a meaningful comeback run. The bottom line is the Sixers are in a different class than the Bulls, who fell to 1-6.

As for the Sixers, they'll wake up Tuesday morning at the top of the Eastern Conference standings.

"You've got some of these nights where you kind of call them professional wins. …You look at the records and all that kind of stuff, but they have a lot of talent," Nurse said. "A lot of these teams play a way that's a little scary. It's fast and it's furious, lots of threes. … They're going to keep coming at you, high-possession games. So they're not easy games and I'm glad that we handled it professionally tonight."

#2811. Lakers Get Major Adou Thiero Boost, But New Injury Concern Emerges Before Blazers Game

INGLEWOOD, CALIFORNIA - OCTOBER 28: Sandro Mamukelashvili #54 of the Los Angeles Lakers looks to pass during the second half of a game against the LA Clippers at Intuit Dome on October 28, 2026 in Inglewood, California. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Sean M. Haffey/Getty Images) Getty

The Los Angeles Lakers are finally getting healthier, but their latest injury report brought one unexpected concern.

Second-year forward Adou Thiero is no longer listed on the team's injury report ahead of Friday's NBA Cup matchup against the Portland Trail Blazers, clearing the way for his potential season debut.

However, reserve big man Sandro Mamukelashvili has been listed as questionable because of an illness, according to the NBA's official injury report released Thursday night.

The developments come as Los Angeles looks to extend its three-game winning streak following Wednesday's 116-103 victory over the Sacramento Kings.

Thiero's removal from the injury report represents a significant step forward after he missed the Lakers' first nine games because of recurring left knee problems.

The former Arkansas standout dealt with swelling in the same knee that required surgery in 2025.

His recovery included a recent assignment to the Coachella Valley Lakers, where he participated in a live practice Wednesday.

Lakers coach JJ Redick confirmed the session went according to plan.

"Practice was fine, went well," Redick told reporters before Wednesday's victory in Sacramento. "That was the plan, just to get him a live practice today and then he'll be back with us."

Redick had previously expressed optimism about Thiero's progress.

"He's had some live exposures," Redick said. "And I think he's trending in the right direction."

Thursday's injury report provides the strongest indication yet that Thiero is ready to return.

However, his absence from the report doesn't guarantee playing time Friday.

Thiero appeared in 25 games as a rookie last season, averaging 1.9 points and 1.1 rebounds in limited minutes.

The Lakers hope his athleticism and defensive versatility will eventually provide another option on the wing.

GettySandro Mamukelashvili of the Los Angeles Lakers looks to pass during the second half of a game against the LA Clippers at Intuit Dome on October 28, 2026 in Inglewood, California.

While Thiero moves closer to returning, Mamukelashvili could miss his first game of the season.

The 27-year-old forward-center has become a dependable contributor in Redick's second unit.

Through nine appearances, Mamukelashvili is averaging 9.0 points, 4.6 rebounds and 1.7 assists in approximately 23 minutes per game.

He delivered one of his strongest performances against Portland on Oct. 27, scoring a season-high 17 points while making three 3-pointers.

More recently, Mamukelashvili contributed 13 points and four assists in Monday's victory over Miami.

However, he struggled offensively Wednesday against Sacramento, finishing with two points on 0-for-4 shooting while collecting six rebounds.

His possible absence could create additional opportunities for Jarred Vanderbilt and Jake LaRavia.

Mamukelashvili's uncertain status presents Redick with another rotation decision just as Thiero becomes available.

The two forwards offer different strengths.

Mamukelashvili provides frontcourt size, rebounding and perimeter shooting, while Thiero's value centers on his athleticism and defensive versatility.

Still, expecting Thiero to immediately replace Mamukelashvili's production would be premature after such a lengthy absence.

Meanwhile, Bronny James and Cameron Carr are unavailable because of their assignments to Coachella Valley for its season opener.

Los Angeles hosts Portland at Crypto.com Arena on Friday at 7 p.m. Pacific.

Thiero's return would give Redick another healthy option.

Whether the Lakers can also count on Mamukelashvili remains uncertain.

#2810. Back surgery will keep Robert Kraft from Rob Gronkowski's Patriots Hall of Fame induction

Patriots owner Robert Kraft recently underwent back surgery, which will keep him sidelined on “injured reserve” and force him to miss Rob Gronkowski’s induction into the Patriots Hall of Fame on Saturday.

“As much pain as I was in before having surgery, it pains me even more to miss the ceremony,” Kraft, 85, told Mike Reiss of ESPN on Thursday.

Gronkowski, having undergone multiple back surgeries during his playing career, understood Kraft’s absence, the owner said.

“I always told Gronk that in my next life, I wanted to come back as him, because I had never seen him have a bad day. But after a couple of back surgeries, I take that back. I never want to experience that again,” Kraft said.

“I think this year compared to other years there were quite a lot of candidates to win the Ballon d’Or. What I liked is that the one [Kane] who won didn’t advertise for themselves,” the Dutch striker said at a media round attended by Bulinews.com.

“I think [Kane’s action in the build up] was the best approach. It was really nice to see him win it and in my eyes he deserved it. If you score 73 goals, win the [German] double and get third-place at the World Cup [with England]

#2808. As Chargers' offense struggles, Mike McDaniel will move from the sideline to the booth

Chargers offensive coordinator Mike McDaniel will get a different perspective on his struggling offense on Sunday against the Texans.

McDaniel said today that he will move upstairs to the booth to call plays after previously working from the sideline.

“One of the things that I want to try is call plays from the box to see adjustments for myself and communicate with the position coaches appropriately what things we can focus on between series,” McDaniel said. “I won’t be in the same spot.”

Although the Chargers’ offense has been among the worst in the NFL this season and head coach Jim Harbaugh has faced questions about whether he should fire his offensive coordinator, McDaniel insisted that he’s not concerned about the criticism.

“My problem solving doesn’t come from insecurity,” McDaniel said. “I’m not trying to prove or justify anything.”

The Chargers rank dead last in the NFL in scoring, having scored only 99 points this season, and they have an NFL-high 17 turnovers. McDaniel said he thinks it’s worth trying something different while delegating some of what he had been doing on the sideline to the assistant coaches.

“Seems like a good action to take based on how things have gone this year, and realistically something that I’ve only brought up because I’ve observed, I’m very confident in the rest of the offensive coaching staff and how they’re able to set the tone and communicate information on the sidelines. So I feel a little more comfortable to try that,” McDaniel said.

If that doesn’t work, Harbaugh’s next step might be to move McDaniel from the booth to the unemployment line.

#2807. ‘I'm finding my footing': Jaylen Brown's role taking shape with Sixers

'I'm finding my footing': Jaylen Brown's role taking shape with Sixers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown has spent the opening weeks of the season describing the Sixers as a work in progress.

He offered a similarly humble perspective Monday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena. His performance, however, provided the clearest look yet at how he can influence this group while sharing the floor with the club's other established stars.

Brown finished with 25 points, 11 rebounds and five assists in the Sixers' 126-107 win over the Chicago Bulls. He shot 9 for 15 from the field, made four three-pointers and did not commit a turnover.

Five of his rebounds came on the offensive end, an area Brown has emphasized while the Sixers continue piecing together their frontcourt rotation.

"Just a mindset," Brown said. "Just trying to create extra opportunities for my team. As we're trying to patch together our fives and our front line, I try to make an extra contribution and emphasis on the glass."

Brown has also been careful about how he describes his place on a roster that already belonged to Joel Embiid and Tyrese Maxey.

Maxey scored 15 of his 26 points in the fourth quarter Monday, continuing his torrid start to the season. He has led the Sixers in scoring while being the only starter to appear in all eight games.

Brown is not interested in interrupting that.

"Maxey's been leading the way, 100 percent," Brown said. "He's been here. This is his team. Him and Joel have been here for a while, so the mindset has been to complement those guys. I'm finding my footing, but Maxey has been leading the way for sure."

Brown's footing looked firm against Chicago.

Beyond his scoring, he helped the Sixers control the glass, moved the ball within an offense that generated 23 assists during the first half and supplied the physicality Nick Nurse expects from him.

"The dude is tough and he wins," Nurse said. "I'll take that all day long. The degree of difficulty on some of the shots he hit was really amazing. I thought he pounded the glass and got some key rebounds. Makes a lot of winning plays. Man, he's a winner."

Brown spent the first 10 years of his career in Boston. He won a championship there, reached another NBA Finals and grew from a 20-year-old prospect into one of the league's premier two-way wings.

Moving into a new system alongside James, Embiid and Maxey was never going to be seamless.

"I mean, he's fitting in along with all of us," James said. "We're getting better and better. It's not going to happen overnight. He spent his first 10 years in a system in Boston, in the city, so it's going to take some adjustment.

"It takes adjustment for him. It's going to take adjustment for the rest of the guys that joined this franchise as well. But tonight was a great step."

James pointed to Brown's "pressure, his shot-making ability, his rebounding ability" and his professionalism.

Brown's early influence has extended beyond his production.

The Sixers entered Monday with one of the NBA's best half-court defenses. Brown has been one of the group's most persistent voices, stressing communication, preparation and attention to opposing players' tendencies.

"That's the part that I care about the most as a group, not individually," Brown said. "Having a mentality night in and night out, respecting your opponent, respecting the game, making sure that we are executing our coverages, we're being clear in our communication and then we're also taking away tendencies on a night-to-night basis.

"Those things I just explained are over 50 percent of the battle."

Brown understands that maintaining those details across an 82-game schedule will require more than reminders.

"A little bit of accountability," he said. "You learn different guys require different things. How you communicate with each guy may be a little different, but accountability for sure and confrontation if need be.

"The offense is going to be what it's going to be, but our defense has got to be non-negotiable. That's something I say all the time. Non-negotiable. You want to be a good team, defense is non-negotiable."

The Sixers showed both sides of their defensive potential Monday.

They built a double-digit lead during the first half, allowed Chicago to score 36 points in the second quarter and then created separation after halftime. Philadelphia held the Bulls to 20 points in the third quarter before pulling away in the fourth.

Brown did not celebrate their ability to raise their intensity after it slipped.

"I don't like that we have that," he said. "It's good that we have shown the potential, but we've got to keep that switch on. It doesn't work like that where you just flip the switch.

"It can catch up to us and it can bite us against a better team or on a night that we're not being as crisp on offense. It can't be a switch just because we're playing a lesser opponent or whatever the case may be. We've got to make sure that mentality is from the beginning to end. Can't skip any steps."

That urgency accompanies considerable optimism.

Brown said the Sixers enjoy playing together. He loves when James grabs a rebound because "he's like a quarterback," capable of launching an outlet pass or immediately pushing the pace.

The group is learning when to attack, when to move the ball and how to avoid hesitating in its effort to play unselfishly.

"It's a lot of talent, man," Brown said. "We like each other, and we're just kind of building that chemistry of how we can get this thing to a well-oiled machine. I think we're taking steps forward every single day."

The Sixers are 7-1 despite Brown believing those steps remain incomplete. They have won close games, erased sizable deficits and received important contributions from different players.

Brown's performance Monday showed another version of how their pieces can fit.

"We still have a long way to go to being a really, really solid, great team," Brown said. "But we've been able to win some games. We started out the season great, 7-1, and you haven't seen our best version yet.

#2805. Bradshaw Defends Aikman As Money Drives MNF Feud

Terry Bradshaw refuses to accept the nasty rumors swirling around his former broadcast partners. The Hall of Famer knows both men well and sees a different story than the one playing out in headlines. He reached out to Joe Buck directly to clear the air before the Monday night show even aired.

Bradshaw didn't wait for press releases or vague leaks to form his opinion; he went straight to the source. "I talked to Buck via text and told him I don't assume everything I'm reading is correct," Bradshaw said. This direct contact shows the old guard still values their bond over the drama. Bradshaw worked with the duo at Fox Sports for twenty years before they moved to ESPN. He knows their characters better than any reporter sitting in a studio.

The reports claim the split is purely about cash. Bradshaw admits the money angle is the main topic of conversation right now. "(The reporting has been) it's about money," Bradshaw revealed. Buck signed a massive six-year deal worth around $108 million to stay at ESPN. Aikman has been waiting for his own contract talks to finish. The Athletic reported that Aikman's side sought a new deal in the same neighborhood as Buck's and was rebuffed, with ESPN pausing talks until after the Super Bowl. That pay gap seems to be the root of the tension.

Even with the dollar signs flashing, Bradshaw thinks the narrative is wrong. He sees Troy Aikman as a man who would never act out of petty spite. "I find that hard to believe because Troy Aikman is not like that. He's not like that. My understanding right now is things are okay," Bradshaw said. The broadcasts have continued without any visible on-screen issues. The chemistry still works for the viewers even if the business side is messy.

The timing of Buck's deal seems to be the real problem. Bradshaw explained that the two men were supposed to enter ESPN as a package. "I have heard that they were going to go into ESPN together and get a new deal together," Bradshaw added. The fact that Buck got his contract first created a rift where none had existed before. It wasn't a lack of friendship that caused the split, but a lack of coordination.

Greg Olsen has been mentioned as the potential replacement for the booth. If the partnership ends, the 2027 Super Bowl could be their last game together. That is a long way to go for a team that just signed a new deal. Bradshaw's voice carries weight because he has seen these deals before.

#2803. Observations after Sixers earn strong road win over Pistons, Brown scores 30

Observations after Sixers earn strong road win over Pistons, Brown scores 30 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers remain the Eastern Conference's sole leader.

They earned a 112-106 road win over the Pistons on Wednesday night to move to 8-1 on the season and stay at the top of the standings. Detroit fell to 6-2.

The Sixers last started 8-1 in the 2023-24 season, which was Nick Nurse's first as the team's head coach.

Jaylen Brown scored a season-high 30 points and pulled down seven rebounds.

Joel Embiid had 26 points and a season-best 15 rebounds. LeBron James posted 13 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists. Tyrese Maxey added 19 points and seven assists.

The Pistons' leading scorers were Duncan Robinson with 27 points and Cade Cunningham with 26.

The Sixers were down Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Pistons' injured players included Jalen Duren (right hamstring strain) and Isaiah Joe (right hip contusion).

Nurse told reporters in Detroit he thinks "it'll be another 10 to 12 days before they kind of clear (Barlow) for getting on the court to see where he is," according to Sixers Wire's Ky Carlin.

The Sixers will visit the Cavaliers on Friday night. Here are observations on their victory over Detroit:

Embiid assisted the Sixers' first basket with a sharp pass out of a double team. He saw Brown's cut along the baseline and dished to him for a layup.

Detroit ran several double drag screen actions early, forcing Embiid to defend on the perimeter. The Pistons started 7 for 11 from the floor and took a 17-11 edge on a Robinson three-pointer.

The Sixers then made a 10-0 run and Embiid was right in the middle of it. He converted a put-back layup after a pretty high-low feed from James. Embiid hit two foul shots to give the Sixers the lead. The team raised its defensive level and Detroit's offense grew frigid.

Embiid was very good on the glass in the first half, grabbing eight rebounds. The Sixers had an excellent rebounding game as a team, limiting the Pistons to four offensive boards over 48 minutes.

Embiid didn't break out of his early-season three-point shooting slump, going 0 for 3. He's 3 for 26 (11.5 percent) on the season and hasn't made a three since the Sixers' second game.

Late in the second quarter, Embiid got into a dead-ball dust-up with Tolu Smith. After an officials' review, Embiid was assessed a technical foul for "initiating" the altercation.

Playing on his 26th birthday, Maxey missed his first four field goals and was scoreless until he hit a floater with 31.5 seconds left in the first quarter.

Brown picked up the scoring slack, tallying 10 points in the first on 4-for-6 shooting and hitting a deep, buzzer-beating three to put the Sixers up 29-21. He produced at all three levels, chose smart spots to attack and looked comfortable in the rhythm of the Sixers' offense.

Meanwhile, James did his usuall stellar work as a passer. He assisted two Adem Bona slams in the second quarter and constantly spotted open teammates. Nurse rewarded Bona's strong opening stint, playing him for a lengthy stretch.

Brown swished a corner triple to extend the Sixers' lead to 48-31. With a 3-for-5 night, Brown is at 45.7 percent from long distance so far this season. It wouldn't be surprising at all if Brown's three-point shooting improved from last season's 34.7 percent clip. With such an abundance of star power, the Sixers' hope will be that everyone gets regular open looks beyond the arc throughout the year.

As for Maxey, he sat with 3:26 left in the second quarter after being whistled for his third foul. Even with a mere two points from Maxey, the Sixers entered halftime holding a 14-point advantage.

The Pistons played better basketball to begin the third quarter.

Following a John Collins alley-oop jam, Nurse called timeout with his team's lead down to single digits. The Pistons' offense sustained some success, but the Sixers prevented a sustained run.

Embiid played an especially physical, aggressive third quarter. He tipped in his own miss and Maxey then sunk a three to make the Sixers' lead 84-68. They were up as many as 17 points in the third.

Embiid sat out the fourth quarter Monday night for the Sixers' win over the Bulls and said he was on a minutes restriction (25) because of his right foot plantar fasciitis. He played the fourth period against the Pistons and logged 33 minutes. Bona went back to the locker room early in the fourth. Nurse told reporters postgame that Bona had "neck soreness."

Detroit did not let the Sixers coast to victory.

Robinson drained a three. Cunningham made a pair of free throws and drove in for a layup that cut the Pistons' deficit to 106-102.

The Sixers halted the Pistons' run before anything disastrous was on the table. Brown blocked a Cunningham three-point try, Embiid knocked down two foul shots, and the East's No. 1 early-season team improved to 4-0 on the road.
